| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# Background (not graded): `launch-global-state-question.md`

# Shared global state at native launch boundaries

Round-2 finding `bp-acm` requires a contract decision about the native caller
adapter. This document requests that decision; it does not adopt a new protocol
or claim that the bug is fixed.

Dialect 0.2.10 §4.3 requires one classical memory shared by H, CC, R and L
entry state. Section 19.4 preserves native H callers unless pointer-rich sharing
requires promotion. The mixed-object requirement `bennett-4ht` also preserves
ordinary native callers and multiple selected module entries.

The ISA 0.8 §10 C API supplies program/run ownership, typed arguments/results,
host requests, reversal and close/unload. Its published header has no operation
to bind, import or export a classical memory range. A layout image supplies
initial data, not shared native storage. Using a native pointer as an ISA
integer address does not connect the two memories.

The regression `tests/review_regressions.py --major M --case global-counter`
links the emitted host bitcode with the published VM library and tests three
observations on both LLVM 18 and 23:

| Native caller actions | Required output | Current output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Call `top` twice | `1 2 host_global=2` | `1 1 host_global=0` |
| Call `top`, set native `g=40`, call again | `1 41 host_global=41` | `1 1 host_global=40` |
| Call `top`, then another marked entry sharing `g` | `1 2 host_global=2` | `1 1 host_global=0` |

The first row alone could tempt a program cache. A cache still leaves native
`g` unchanged and cannot import the intervening H write. Separate caches for
entries also retain separate device copies of the same source global. Neither
is a fix for §4.3. Promoting every native caller would change §19.4 placement.

The requested decision is the supported classical-state bridge for this
boundary: may the producer publish a versioned copy-in/writeback service using
existing ISA host requests, or should the launch contract supply explicit
memory-transfer/binding operations? Existing integer arguments/results could
also carry bytes through a generated adapter, but that would still need a
published mapping and ownership convention, not an unchecked host-pointer cast.

Whichever route is selected, its publication must specify:

- Source global identity, selected component ownership and native/device
  address correspondence, including globals shared by several entries.
- Copy-in of current H bytes and copy-out of device writes, including aliases,
  with the §19.4 promotion rule for pointer-rich reachable storage.
- Program context lifetime, compatible component linking, and handle/token
  namespaces; a function-local static cache cannot decide these for the native
  container.
- Memory ownership while a run is active, writeback of completed or faulted
  prefixes, and how reversal restores the host-visible state before subsequent
  H mutation or reuse.
- The adapter service/schema or C API, and the component metadata that lets
  plugin, driver, runtime and executor independently implement the same bridge.

This requests the smallest bridge needed to implement the existing shared
memory semantics. It does not request whole-process-only placement, private
access to VM memory, a change to the ISA 0.8 inner object grammar, or a silent
ISA upgrade. The general mixed-object audit is deferred; the determining
blocker here is the concrete P0 loss of an ordinary shared scalar global.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# F04/F08/F23 — native calls and registered memory

Beads: **b-isa-spec-x1y**, **b-isa-spec-6ma**, **bennett-vm-8v0** (contract side only). This is an amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4. F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands. No launch ABI or backend is implemented here.

## Exact defect and clause citations

[ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md) §10 binds INT/F64/QREG/TOKEN to device CC parameters and copies argument/entropy buffers. It has neither an ordinary `ptr` binding nor an aggregate/native calling convention. §§7.1–7.2 initialise device-owned memory and allocation metadata from `layout`; an integer holding native pointer bits supplies neither liveness nor access authority. §0.3's deferred ISA-11 cannot substitute for a published native-memory contract.

[AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F04/F08/F23 correctly identifies a mixed-case lock-out. The existing by-value launch works within its stated domain; this is not a reproduced failure of a host-sharing backend. F23's actual bead now cites [VM PLAN, proposal 0.1](../../../../bennett-vm/docs/PLAN.md), which already sketches registrations, leases and eleven future vectors. That design is useful input, not a published cross-repository interface or executed integration evidence. No duplicate F23 implementation note is needed.

## North Star gap

This is a real missing boundary capability for North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4: native main must call marked modules while preserving ordinary pointer-rich sharing. Promoting main or copying unrelated buffers cannot be the only eventual realization. Existing rungs and device lowering remain valid and independent of this amendment.

## Alternatives and witnesses

| Construction | Witness and consequence |
|---|---|
| Treat `ptr` as INT | Native address 0x1000 can denote unmapped device storage, a different global, or stale storage after reuse. Width agreement supplies no ownership. Unsound as registration. |
| Snapshot into independent buffers | Two native pointers to one interior cell must observe the same write; two independent copies can disagree. Useful only with an explicit alias-preserving import/export protocol. |
| Promote all pointer-sharing callers | Expresses device computation but converts native main in the cyclic graph case. Preserve as an option; it does not witness the mixed capability. |
| Versioned registration and native adapter | Canonical allocation identities, alias-preserving address observations and leases support native calls and reversal. Recommended. Same-address and imported-private-oracle profiles have distinct evidence. |

Concrete acceptance witness: native bytes `[10,20,30,40]`; call A adds 3 to byte 1 and closes, yielding `[10,23,30,40]`. Native code sets byte 0 to 99. Call B XORs byte 0 with 1; reversing B **before close** restores `[99,23,30,40]`. A stale first-call snapshot would incorrectly restore 10. A second witness has two cyclic nodes and two arguments to one interior value: write 7 through one alias, read 7 through the other, preserve pointer equality and address-derived outputs. The finite model in [verify.py](verify.py) checks the bookkeeping and alias counterexample; it is not VM execution.

## Recommended exact spec diff

Append to §10 Model, and cross-reference from §§7.1–7.2:

```text
Native process entry and device CC entry are separate roles. A mixed
container MAY expose a native callable symbol whose adapter selects a
device program and CC entry. launch 0.9's existing argument kinds remain
by-value; integer bits do not register native storage. Native calls with
ordinary pointers or aggregates use an explicitly negotiated mixed-launch
and memory profile. The following requirements govern that profile.

The adapter descriptor binds container component/local-entry, linked ISA
object_hash, entry signature and closure_hash, target ABI/triple/DataLayout,
layout/mapping profile, certificate/build record, and runtime/service
versions. It specifies positional scalar and aggregate decomposition,
field offsets, padding treatment (unobservable padding is zero-filled),
endianness, integer widths and exact float bits. Data pointers, function
pointers, opaque launch ids and tokens have distinct descriptor kinds.
Pointers are capabilities plus checked offsets; raw integers grant no
native access. Null and one-past values retain their source semantics;
one-past is not a dereferenceable byte.

A registration records canonical allocation identity and generation,
owner/liveness authority, extent, permissions, target layout and mapping,
allocator metadata policy, and current entry epoch. Interior/overlapping
views resolve to that same identity. Mapping preserves aliasing and all
defined pointer observations, including comparisons, ptrtoint/address
hashing and indirect dispatch; it MUST NOT silently swizzle unidentified
integer words. A same-address profile requires matching representation and
nonconflicting ranges. Other mappings require a separately published
observation-preserving encoding/relocation protocol, not a capability cap.

Before access, validate nonwrapping ranges, generation, liveness, layout,
permissions and ownership. Device-private allocations keep §7.1 semantics.
Imported storage supplies equivalent bounds/liveness/allocation metadata;
it does not require native malloc to have executed the device bump history.
Escaping allocations require a host-compatible allocation/transfer profile
covering native free/realloc, aliases, budgets and reversible metadata.
A private device address alone cannot become a returned native pointer.

For the exclusive mutable profile, acquire a coordinated lease before the
run and retain it through CLOSED. All aliases and programs sharing that
storage participate in the same coordinator. Host/free/realloc/unregister
and conflicting runs cannot access it during the lease; compatible read
leases and disjoint storage may coexist. The liveness authority must
prevent or detect invalidation; hashing entry bytes is not an interference
protocol for arbitrary unsynchronised native writes.

Each invocation binds program/root/run, arguments, registration generations
and entry epochs. COMPLETED/FAILED retain ownership. Eligible reverse
restores this invocation's entry bytes and allocator/ownership history
before close, retaining §10's measurement/free restrictions. A failed
operation makes no partial change; earlier successful operations remain
in the failed prefix and reverse under §§7.4/10. Close commits the exact
remaining prefix and releases leases. This is synchronised visibility,
not atomic publication of an arbitrary graph. After close a later native
edit is part of the next invocation's entry state.

The synchronous adapter waits through HOST_REQUEST and suspension under
§10, distinguishes API failure from a FAILED device result/fault, extracts
declared results, closes and returns to the native caller. An asynchronous
adapter returns an opaque run owner; it does not grant early access to
leased memory. No exception crosses the C boundary implicitly. Profile
negotiation, stale/wrong-owner/unregistered pointer, range/layout mismatch,
interference and failed-operation diagnostics are distinct published
status outcomes; assign their wire encodings with the new ABI version.
Do not allocate numeric tags in launch 0.9's existing enum.

Lifted/oracle memory is a private coherent construction. Import a
consistent entry image and equivalent metadata before private allocation,
then retain the prescribed forward/reverse semantics. A quantum address
does not cause a native RAM read. Current copied launches and snapshot/
replay remain admitted constructions, not the sole eventual native-sharing
capability. Device effects, CC replies and native I/O retain separate owners.
```

Publish the descriptor and C wire layouts as one versioned companion contract before implementing it in driver/runtime/VM. The text deliberately leaves numeric encodings for that publication, rather than presenting a partial C header as ready to consume. F23 should consume this publication and the F15/F16 mapping rules; retain the VM's existing integer-address backend and by-value ABI meanwhile.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Runtime registrations/addresses belong to invocation records, never immutable ISA source identities. Container bytes/digest, payload bytes/digest, `object_hash`, root closure, certificate and runtime profile are separate bindings. Changed imported images/layouts or generated wrappers can change actual object/closure/schedule/certificate hashes. A memory profile alone does not change `ref@0.9` recipe bytes. Driver, runtimes, VM, plugin and conformance must pin the accepted contract independently; no consumer imports VM source as the protocol.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

Require scalar/F64/QREG/TOKEN compatibility; aggregate padding and float-bit round trips; both concrete witnesses above; interior/overlapping/cyclic aliases; host edits between closed runs; free/reuse at the same address; null/one-past/range overflow; stale/wrong-program generations; conflicting leases; failed-store prefix and reverse; measurement ineligibility; returned host-owned allocations and native realloc; function pointers; two embedded roots; lifted private import and metadata-only invalidation. Compare the same materialised device closure and layout, preserving original pointer identity for address-sensitive baselines. Unregistered numeric CLI pointers and `--mem` hex copies are negative evidence controls. These are proposed integration vectors, not claims of an implemented backend. [INDEX](INDEX.md) records the actual bounded checks.

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

Recommend a versioned companion `mixed-launch/memory` contract, normatively referenced from ISA 0.10 and the next dialect revision. A 0.9 K entry may clarify the existing by-value boundary, but cannot add pointer tags or memory semantics silently. Any in-version K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including negotiation and status encodings. Assign numeric K ids only on acceptance. No recipe catalog change is required by this design alone.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F04/F08/F23. Native main, argv/envp and host-only libc remain native; only marked modules and their complete device closures enter ISA inventory. Each root keeps its own certificate/context `[]`, budget/ledger and run/tape association. Shared storage uses explicit coordination across those roots. This keeps the mixed fat object reachable; it neither implements it nor makes its amendments prerequisites for rungs 3/4. F30 stays deferred.

codex
I’ll read the proposal, the affected ISA clauses, and the cited verification material, then grade the diff against the shared-state observations and mixed-object requirements.

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

device program and CC entry. launch 0.9's existing argument kinds remain
by-value; integer bits do not register native storage. Native calls with
ordinary pointers or aggregates use an explicitly negotiated mixed-launch
and memory profile. The following requirements govern that profile.

object_hash, entry signature and closure_hash, target ABI/triple/DataLayout,
layout/mapping profile, certificate/build record, and runtime/service

pointers, opaque launch ids and tokens have distinct descriptor kinds.

native access. Null and one-past values retain their source semantics;
one-past is not a dereferenceable byte.

allocator metadata policy, and current entry epoch. Interior/overlapping

hashing and indirect dispatch; it MUST NOT silently swizzle unidentified

permissions and ownership. Device-private allocations keep §7.1 semantics.
Imported storage supplies equivalent bounds/liveness/allocation metadata;
it does not require native malloc to have executed the device bump history.
Escaping allocations require a host-compatible allocation/transfer profile
covering native free/realloc, aliases, budgets and reversible metadata.
A private device address alone cannot become a returned native pointer.

For the exclusive mutable profile, acquire a coordinated lease before the
run and retain it through CLOSED. All aliases and programs sharing that
storage participate in the same coordinator. Host/free/realloc/unregister
and conflicting runs cannot access it during the lease; compatible read
leases and disjoint storage may coexist. The liveness authority must
prevent or detect invalidation; hashing entry bytes is not an interference
protocol for arbitrary unsynchronised native writes.

Each invocation binds program/root/run, arguments, registration generations
and entry epochs. COMPLETED/FAILED retain ownership. Eligible reverse
restores this invocation's entry bytes and allocator/ownership history
before close, retaining §10's measurement/free restrictions. A failed
operation makes no partial change; earlier successful operations remain
in the failed prefix and reverse under §§7.4/10. Close commits the exact
remaining prefix and releases leases. This is synchronised visibility,
not atomic publication of an arbitrary graph. After close a later native
edit is part of the next invocation's entry state.

The synchronous adapter waits through HOST_REQUEST and suspension under
§10, distinguishes API failure from a FAILED device result/fault, extracts
declared results, closes and returns to the native caller. An asynchronous
adapter returns an opaque run owner; it does not grant early access to
leased memory. No exception crosses the C boundary implicitly. Profile
negotiation, stale/wrong-owner/unregistered pointer, range/layout mismatch,
interference and failed-operation diagnostics are distinct published
status outcomes; assign their wire encodings with the new ABI version.
Do not allocate numeric tags in launch 0.9's existing enum.

Lifted/oracle memory is a private coherent construction. Import a
consistent entry image and equivalent metadata before private allocation,
then retain the prescribed forward/reverse semantics. A quantum address
does not cause a native RAM read. Current copied launches and snapshot/
replay remain admitted constructions, not the sole eventual native-sharing
capability. Device effects, CC replies and native I/O retain separate owners.
```

Publish the descriptor and C wire layouts as one versioned companion contract before implementing it in driver/runtime/VM. The text deliberately leaves numeric encodings for that publication, rather than presenting a partial C header as ready to consume. F23 should consume this publication and the F15/F16 mapping rules; retain the VM's existing integer-address backend and by-value ABI meanwhile.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Runtime registrations/addresses belong to invocation records, never immutable ISA source identities. Container bytes/digest, payload bytes/digest, `object_hash`, root closure, certificate and runtime profile are separate bindings. Changed imported images/layouts or generated wrappers can change actual object/closure/schedule/certificate hashes. A memory profile alone does not change `ref@0.9` recipe bytes. Driver, runtimes, VM, plugin and conformance must pin the accepted contract independently; no consumer imports VM source as the protocol.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

Require scalar/F64/QREG/TOKEN compatibility; aggregate padding and float-bit round trips; both concrete witnesses above; interior/overlapping/cyclic aliases; host edits between closed runs; free/reuse at the same address; null/one-past/range overflow; stale/wrong-program generations; conflicting leases; failed-store prefix and reverse; measurement ineligibility; returned host-owned allocations and native realloc; function pointers; two embedded roots; lifted private import and metadata-only invalidation. Compare the same materialised device closure and layout, preserving original pointer identity for address-sensitive baselines. Unregistered numeric CLI pointers and `--mem` hex copies are negative evidence controls. These are proposed integration vectors, not claims of an implemented backend. [INDEX](INDEX.md) records the actual bounded checks.

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

Recommend a versioned companion `mixed-launch/memory` contract, normatively referenced from ISA 0.10 and the next dialect revision. A 0.9 K entry may clarify the existing by-value boundary, but cannot add pointer tags or memory semantics silently. Any in-version K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including negotiation and status encodings. Assign numeric K ids only on acceptance. No recipe catalog change is required by this design alone.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F04/F08/F23. Native main, argv/envp and host-only libc remain native; only marked modules and their complete device closures enter ISA inventory. Each root keeps its own certificate/context `[]`, budget/ledger and run/tape association. Shared storage uses explicit coordination across those roots. This keeps the mixed fat object reachable; it neither implements it nor makes its amendments prerequisites for rungs 3/4. F30 stays deferred.

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
python3 -I -B - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md')
source = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(source, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

# Owned deliverable structure and local links.
base = Path('docs/notes')
ids = ['z4c', 'l2p', 'fd0', 'lpe', 'adg', 'xv4', 'b5b', '5u3',
       'checker-native-model', 'checker-diagnostics', 'checker-fault-rate',
       'checker-resource-contexts']
for ident in ids:
    path = base / ('2026-11-02-contract-' + ident + '.md')
    body = path.read_text()
    for word in ['Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                 'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes',
                 'Conformance', 'Release']:
        assert word.lower() in body.lower(), (ident, word)
    assert not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', body)
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in body.splitlines()) % 2 == 0
    for target in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', body):
        if not target.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#')):
            assert (path.parent / target.split('#')[0]).exists(), (ident, target)
print('PASS: 12 proposals; exact AND 8 columns/32 inverse columns; '
      '128 corrected branch columns; 3 negative channels; '
      'model/domain/orbit/resource arithmetic; fences and local links')
print('Fixed-image SHA-256:', image_hash)

## Verification limits

The exact checks establish the finite witnesses and internal document
consistency. They do not prove the proposed general contract sound in
all cases, accept a new K entry, close an implementation bead, or show
that SQLite's full reference analysis converges in practical time.
Each item states its own remaining acceptance vectors and consumer work.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the ISA is

A program model for classical, reversible and coherent computation with
one semantics, executed by the VM (`bennett-vm`) and the circuit
specialiser (`bennett-circuits`), produced by the plugin
(`bennett-plugin`) through the dialect's reference semantics, scheduled
through §11, described for hardware by target descriptions (§8). Nothing
here refers to LLVM.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Executor | VM, specialiser, hardware stacks | §3–§7, §14 exactly, and the checking of inverse-pair claims (§4.5, §12.1; a certificate applies when its `closure_hash` binding equals the region's); suites A–E, H |
| Producer | plugin, library compilers | well-formed objects (§9, §16) and regions (§5, §6); suite F |
| Scheduler | `bennett-sched` and competitors | §11; only schedules valid under §6; suite H |
| Checker | `bennett-conformance` | validates certificates (§12) without trusting producers |
| Target author | vendors | target descriptions (§8); suite E |

### 1.3 Deferred *(asserted non-semantic)*

Binary encodings (§16 text is the interchange form); mechanisation of
§14; device models beyond §7.3; the gate-name registry process; the
memory layout of the opaque C objects of §10–§11 (every constructor,
accessor and destructor is listed). **Minimality claims** (north star
§5) are not supported by cert 0.9 and MUST NOT appear in a certificate.
Decisions deferred to implementation, each with its default, are listed
in §17.

## 2. Terminology

- **Tier.** CC, R, Q. **Wire**, **bundle** `wire[N]` (little-endian).
- **Location** `reg[N]`: a named mutable cell of an oracle or lproc
  register file. **Pseudo-location**: an oracle interface input or
  classical argument, or an lproc classical parameter (§3.7), readable
  only in its declaring function, never written.
- **Region**: a Q function with a declared interface: **lifted oracle**
  (§5.2) or **quantum action** (§5.3); **permutation action** (§5.3).
- **lproc**: a lifted procedure with its own params and locations; it
  resolves names only to its own params and locations (§3.7).
- **Budget**: classical (B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit).
- **Frame**: per machine step: `old_pc`, slots (assignment, capture and
  delivery slots, §3.6), `flag`, `succ`.
- **Basis configuration**: classical values on every component of D.
- **Gate classes.** Permutation: `x cx ccx mcx swap`. Diagonal: `s sdg t
  tdg cz rz`. Non-diagonal: `h rx ry`.
- **Verifying executor**: an executor that can decide exactly whether a
  set of wires is clean on every basis configuration (a state-vector or
  branch-enumeration simulator); every other executor (hardware) is
  **non-verifying**. Rules that depend on the difference say which
  applies (§3.3 `qreg.free`, §10, §17 item 14).
- **Effective control context** at a point: the set of wires on the
  active predicate stack plus the inherited control context (the control
  wires of every enclosing `ctl` and predicate stack of every enclosing
  invocation, transitively); duplicates count once. Its size is the
  **effective control count**.

## 3. Machine model

### 3.1 Tiers and the control invariant

```
  CC ──calls──▶ R functions (call/uncall)       CC ──calls──▶ Q regions
  R  ──calls──▶ R functions, Q regions          Q  ──calls──▶ Q regions (§5.5); oracle bodies call lprocs
```

CC runs on the device's classical-control processor; the host reaches
the device only through §10. **No instruction in any tier makes a
control or scheduling decision that depends on the value of a wire**
except through predication (§3.5). Gate angles are finite `f64` literals
or classical arguments fixed at region entry.

**Call-tier policy** (suite F). The diagram, completed with same-tier
calls; a call, uncall or region call outside this table is ill-formed:

| Caller | `call` (§4.1) | `uncall` of a completed segment (§7.4) | region calls (§4.7) | lifted calls (§4.2) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC function | CC and R functions | of a CC or R callee | oracles, actions | – |
| R function | R functions | of an R callee | oracles, actions | – |
| oracle body, lproc | – | – | – | `lift.call` of lprocs; `lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall` of oracles; `lift.perm_call` of permutation actions |
| action | – | – | oracles, actions (§5.3, §5.5) | – |

An R function never calls a CC function: CC code may measure and make
host requests (§4.1), which an R history cannot reverse.

### 3.2 State

| Component | Tier | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| classical environment | CC, R | dynamic SSA instances (§7.4), block parameters, activation stack, **quantum-register handles** (`qreg[n]`, `qreg[*]`, §3.3), tokens (§5.4) |
| register and token ledgers | CC, R | every register's id, ordered class vector, width and state (§3.3); every device-minted token's tuple and validity (§5.4) |
| classical memory | CC, R | byte-addressed, little-endian, sparse; allocation table (§7.1) |
| effect tape, entropy tape | R | §7.3 |
| run history | CC, R | one frame per executed instruction (§7.4) |
| wire store | Q (CC and R via `qreg`) | live wires with roles `input target fault borrowed mutable workspace location cell` |
| quantum memory | Q | regions of `wire[W]` cells: local (§4.6) or interface (§5.1) |
| register file | Q | locations of the active oracle and its lproc closure (§3.7) |
| predicate stack | Q | wires; the active predicate is their conjunction |
| oracle step state | Q | `pc, pc' : wire[P]`; `faulted : wire`; `code : wire[F]`; `result_val : wire[T]`; counters (§3.7); B frames |
| budget | Q | classical: B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit |

### 3.3 Types, ownership, operand schema

## 7. Memory and effects

### 7.1 Classical memory (CC, R)

Byte-addressed, little-endian, sparse. `layout` partitions the address
space: a **null region** `[0, null_size)` never allocated (null_size ≥
1); globals at fixed bases with images; the heap `[heap_base, heap_base +
heap_limit_max)`, all disjoint, `heap_base + heap_limit_max ≤ 2^ptr_
width`, and the number of globals ≤ 2^A_tab, all validated at load. `p =
alloc n` **fails before any mutation** when bump + max(n, 1) > heap_base
+ heap_limit_max or the table holds 2^A_tab entries (CC trap / R fault
`FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`); otherwise it returns the bump pointer, records
`(base, size = n, live = 1, pinned = 0)` and advances by `max(n, 1)`;
globals have pre-entered pinned live entries; an access is valid iff its
whole byte range lies in one live entry; `free p` marks the unique live
unpinned entry with base p dead (unpinned entries are heap entries,
whose bases strictly increase; pinned entries cannot be freed and a
pinned entry with base p is left unchanged; no live unpinned entry with
base p: CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT`); storage is never
reclaimed.
Initial state: bump = `heap_base`, table = the pre-entries in layout
order, memory zero except images.

### 7.2 Lifted memory of an oracle

`mem` has 2^H byte cells (H ≤ ptr_width); `alloctab` 2^A_tab entries
`(base, size, live, pinned)`; the stack 2^S_cells cells. The `layout`
fixes `null_size`, each global's base and image, `heap_base`, `heap_
limit_max`, and for each **interface region** of the oracle (W = 8
required) a base address; all ranges disjoint and within `[0, 2^H)`, and
for every oracle the number of pre-entries (globals plus its interface
regions) ≤ 2^A_tab, checked at load. `lift.load`/`lift.store` dispatch
by address range to `mem` or to the mapped interface region (role `in`
preserved by the construction). The low H bits address cells; `inrange`
guarantees the range lies in a live entry, hence within `[0, 2^H)`.

**Memoryless objects.** An object without a `layout` section MUST
contain no lprocs, no `lift.addr lift.boundscheck lift.load lift.store
lift.alloc lift.free lift.call lift.ret` rows, no `mem` interface slots
and no CC/R `alloc free load store`; H = A_tab = S_cells = 0, no `mem`,
`alloctab`, stack or counters are allocated, and `bennett_budget`
requires `heap_limit = stack_limit = depth_limit = 0` (§5.4). Example 1
of §16.3 is memoryless.

### 7.3 Device models and tapes (R)

The active effect tape is specified here; persistence and replay of saved execution journals are tool interfaces, whose interchange format and validation behavior are defined by an explicitly versioned replay profile.

Every device operation takes classical-memory operands and writes its
results to memory; each `effect` is **atomic**: validated (operands in
range, tape available) before any change, and a failing validation is a
fault record with no partial delta.

| Device | State (initial) | Operations | Failure | Frame records / reversal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `console` | byte sequence (empty) | `write(ptr, len)` | – | `len`; pop |
| `clock` | `c: u64` (0) | `read(ptr)`: writes c (8 bytes LE) at ptr, then c += 1 | – | overwritten 8 bytes; c −= 1 |
| `entropy` | position `p` (0) into the entropy tape (§10) | `read(ptr, len)`: copies tape[p..p+len) to ptr; p += len | p + len > length: fault `FAULT_ENTROPY_EXHAUSTED` | overwritten bytes; p −= len |
| `block` | sector map from the `block_image` (else empty), 512-byte sectors; absent sectors read as zero | `read(sector, ptr)`; `write(sector, ptr)` | – | read: overwritten 512 bytes; write: the old sector contents **and a presence bit** (absent sectors are removed again on reversal) |

**BLOCK-IMAGE** (K10; source: bennett-vm-47i). `layout.block_image`
names a data symbol (§9.1, §16.1). Decode each successive pair of its
lowercase hex digits to one byte, in textual order. Its decoded length
L MUST be a multiple of 512, including L = 0. At block-device
initialisation, before executing any instruction or changing device
state, reject an image of any other length; never pad or truncate it.
For 0 ≤ s < L/512, sector number s is present and its byte at offset b
(0 ≤ b < 512) is image[512s + b]. There is no sector header, length
prefix, sector-number field or reversal of bytes within a sector. An
all-zero sector in the image is present. Every other sector is absent
and reads as 512 zero bytes; no image and an empty image give an empty
map. This specifies the initial map only; the §7.3 write/reversal
presence-bit rule remains in force. Motivation (0.8 §7.3): "sector map
from the `block_image` (else empty), 512-byte sectors; absent sectors
read as zero"; §9.1: "`data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`". The contiguous
encoding and partial-sector policy are additions, not a claimed 0.8
loader requirement.

**BLOCK-IMAGE witness**

```json
{"length":1024,"present_sectors":[0,1],"sector_first_bytes":[[0,0],[1,255]],"absent_sector":2,"partial_lengths_rejected":[1,511,513,1023]}
```

The image is 512 bytes 00 followed by 512 bytes ff (2048 hex digits).
Sector 0 is present although zero; sector 1 is ff throughout; sector 2
is absent and reads zero. Writing sector 2 then reversing removes it;
writing sector 0 then reversing restores a present zero sector.

Each `effect` appends `(dev, op, args)` to the effect tape.

### 7.4 Run history and the dynamic SSA model (CC, R)

The classical environment holds **dynamic instances**: every executed
defining instruction creates a new instance retained in the run history
until reversal; a name denotes its most recent instance. Frames:

| Instruction | Frame |
|---|---|
| any defining instruction | its defined instance |
| `br` | predecessor block, prior block-parameter instances |
| `store` | address, width, overwritten bytes |
| `alloc` | old bump pointer, the table entry written, the zero-filled range |
| `free` | the entry's old live bit |
| `call` | invocation boundary (callee, argument values) |
| `ret` | result values, the callee's activation and the frame link |
| `uncall` | the reversed segment's id (skipped by whole-run reversal, below) |
| `effect` | tape index plus the device-specific record of §7.3; reversal also removes its effect-tape entry |
| `hostcall` | payload bytes and reply bytes |
| `qreg.*`, `measure` | the ledger record of §3.3 (ids, ordered class vectors, widths, prior states, whether a free's cleanliness was verified) and the measured bits |
| `budget.mint` | the token and its tuple; reversal revokes the token |
| `oracle_call` / `action_call` | (region hash, variant, inherited control count, classical args with every token's tuple and resolved identity mapping, register ids): reversed by re-application / adjoint, exactly once, by this frame; reversal reuses the resolved token identities |
| fault record (a failing instruction, `trap`, `fault`) | cause code (§4.9) and program point; terminal. Reversal (§10) removes the record; the failing instruction had no other effect, so the state is then the state before it |

**Logical history.** Frames and dynamic instances specify the
*logical* history. An executor MAY store it by checkpoints, deltas or
deterministic replay, provided it reconstructs the same logical frames,
instances, reversal marks and traversal order; it never re-issues an
external event (effects are replayed from the effect tape and host
replies from their frames, never requested again), never re-executes a
region call to recover its record, and never copies quantum state. T4 is
stated over the logical history. **Active and audit history.** The
*active* history is the sequence of frames not marked reversed;
reversed frames and `uncall` markers remain as immutable *audit
records*. Undoing a defining frame removes its dynamic instance from the
active environment although its audit record is retained.

**Pure frames.** A frame is **pure** iff it belongs to `br` or to a
defining instruction among `const`, the binops, `udiv..srem`, `icmp`,
`select`, `zext sext trunc`, `ptrtoint inttoptr`, `load`, `qreg.width
qreg.id qreg.from_id`: it changes only the set of instances. Every other
frame (`store alloc free call ret uncall trap fault effect hostcall
qreg.new qreg.free qreg.slice qreg.concat measure budget.mint`, region
calls, fault records) is impure.

**History traversal** (the one reversal procedure, used by segment
`uncall` and by whole-run reversal). A range of frames is **reversed**
by visiting its frames in LIFO order; a frame already marked reversed
and an `uncall` frame are **skipped**; every other frame is undone by
its row of the table above and then marked reversed. Reversed frames
stay in the history, marked, and are never undone again. An `uncall`
marker is always skipped: when a later traversal (an enclosing
segment's `uncall`, or whole-run reversal) reaches it, the frames of the
segment it names are already marked and are skipped too, so neither the
marker nor its segment is undone, re-executed or redone a second time.

**Segment uncall** (`uncall @f (args) (results)`). Let S be the most
recent completed call segment of the history (a `call` frame, its
matching `ret` frame and every frame between them). The `uncall` is
legal iff S is a call of `@f`, S's recorded boundary argument and result
values equal the operand values, **every frame after S is pure**, and S
contains, at any nesting depth, **no `measure` frame** and no `qreg.
free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified (hardware, §17 item 14);
otherwise it is a CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP` and changes nothing
beyond the fault record (§4.9). Execution reverses S by the history traversal (nested segments are
undone; nested segments already cancelled by an inner `uncall`, and that
inner `uncall` frame, are skipped), and pushes an `uncall` frame naming
S. The instances defined inside S no longer exist; using such a name
before it is redefined is a CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`.
**Whole-run reversal** (§10) is the history traversal over the whole
history: S's effects on memory, the allocation table, devices, the
register ledger and tokens were undone by the `uncall`, and the frames between S and the
`uncall` are pure, so at the `uncall` frame every component except the
retained instances already equals its value before S. T4 is stated over
these frames. *(non-normative)* S is never re-executed and never redone;
the compute–copy–`uncall` idiom derives what it keeps through pure
instructions before the `uncall`. Suite A contains: `f` calls `g`,
uncalls `g`, returns; `g` performs one `clock.read`; `call f; uncall f`
leaves the clock at its entry value; and `uncall` of a segment that
measured is a trap.

 succeeded in 0ms:
## 10. Launch ABI (launch 0.9)

Register allocation through `bennett_qreg_new` remains a homogeneous request; §3.3 defines the ordered class vector, concat/slice bindings and complete ledger reversal (K88).

The selected device object/root, fixed-image preparation, target models, resource/frame contexts, borrow proofs and token-alias launch bindings follow §12.3 (K85–K91). In particular its token_aliases mixed-object rule applies at this launch boundary; the general native-container/ordinary-pointer launch contract remains the explicit boundary work named there.

**Model.** A run executes a device CC entry; the host passes classical
arguments, register ids and the entropy tape, receives classical
results, fault codes and declared measurements; host services are
reached by `hostcall`. **Positional binding**: argument i of `bennett_
run` binds to parameter i of the entry's signature: `INT(width, bytes)`
to `iN` with width = N (else `E_ARG`), `F64` to `f64`, `QREG` to `qreg[n]`
with the register's n or to `qreg[*]` with any width, `TOKEN` to `token`
(a valid token, §5.4). Arguments and the entropy tape
are **copied** at `bennett_run`; the caller may free its buffers
afterwards. Host-minted tokens are scoped to the program and destroyed
at unload; device-minted tokens to their run (§5.4).
Accessor-returned pointers are valid until the owning object is freed.
`bennett_unload` with a run not CLOSED: `E_INVALID_STATE`.

**Invocation tree.** A run record is a tree (CC segments, R calls, Q
calls); an R call's subtree is owned by its R history; `bennett_reverse`
reverses post-order: CC segments by their run history, R calls by LIFO
reversal of their history (§7.4, reversed segments and `uncall` frames
skipped), top-level Q calls by re-application / adjoint. A fault record
is terminal (§4.9): the run is `FAILED`, and every activation open at
the fault keeps its frames, which reversal undoes like those of a call
that returned.

**States.** `CREATED → RUNNING → (SUSPENDED ⇄ RUNNING) → (HOST_REQUEST ⇄
RUNNING) → COMPLETED | FAILED`; `COMPLETED → REVERSED`; `FAILED →
REVERSED`; any of
`COMPLETED/FAILED/REVERSED → CLOSED` (`bennett_run_close` releases the
record; register ids created by the run remain valid unless measured or
freed). A run owns the registers and memory it touches exclusively while
not CLOSED; a register reached by `qreg.from_id` is touched from then
on; `bennett_qreg_prepare`, `bennett_qreg_free` and a `bennett_run`
argument naming a register owned by another run that is not CLOSED are
`E_INVALID_STATE`. **Reverse eligibility**: `COMPLETED` or `FAILED`; no `measure`
frame; no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified (§17
item 14); no register it touched has been prepared, freed, sliced or
used by another run since; else `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE`. Reversing a `FAILED`
run first undoes its terminal fault record, then traverses the rest of
its history exactly as for a completed run, including the frames of
activations that never returned; the run is then `REVERSED` with every
component T4 names at its entry value. `bennett_qreg_prepare` and `bennett_
qreg_free` require a clean register (`E_QREG_NOT_CLEAN` otherwise on a
verifying executor; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14); there
is no reset and no trace-out: a register in unknown state is released by
a `measure` inside a run, which consumes its handle (§3.3).

**Results.** `bennett_result_get` writes value i and sets `*kind` and
`*width`: `INT` as ⌈N/8⌉ little-endian bytes, `*width` = N; `F64` as the
8 IEEE 754 binary64 little-endian bytes, `*width` = 64; `QREG` and
`TOKEN` as the 8-byte little-endian id, `*width` = 64. A `cap` smaller
than that byte count is `E_ARG` and nothing is written. Fault records
are read by `bennett_result_nfaults` / `bennett_result_fault` (codes of
§4.9; a `COMPLETED` run has none, a `FAILED` run has exactly one, its
terminal record, and exposes no values).
For an R `fault C`, `bennett_wait` returns `OK` with `*result_out` set
and `*request_out` NULL as for any `FAILED` run;
`bennett_result_nvalues` is 0, `bennett_result_nfaults` is 1 and
`bennett_result_fault(result, 0)` is exactly C when C ≠ 0 (4 when C = 0,
§5.4), with all 64 bits preserved. The fault code is result data, not a
`bennett_status` error or a host-service reply.

**Waiting.** `bennett_wait` blocks until the run leaves `RUNNING` and
returns `OK` with exactly one outcome: `HOST_REQUEST` (`*request_out`
set, `*result_out` NULL; `bennett_reply` answers it and the run is
`RUNNING` again) or `COMPLETED` / `FAILED` (`*result_out` set, `*request_
out` NULL). Waiting on a `SUSPENDED`, `REVERSED` or `CLOSED` run is `E_
INVALID_STATE`.

**`hostcall`.** `rlen = hostcall service ptr len`: `[ptr, ptr + len)` MUST
lie in one live allocation (else CC trap and no request is made); those
bytes are copied into the request; the run enters `HOST_REQUEST`; the
reply payload is written to `[ptr, ptr + min(len, reply length))` and
`rlen` = the full reply length (so truncation is detectable; a reply
whose length does not fit `ptr_width` bits is refused by `bennett_reply`
with `E_ARG` before any write, and the run stays in `HOST_REQUEST`); the frame
records the request bytes, the overwritten bytes and the reply bytes.
Every `service` is declared in `devices.services`; an undeclared one is
a load error.

**Tape.** `bennett_tape_next` yields each `effect` in tape order; `args`
holds the operation's operands in the order of §7.3, each an unsigned
64-bit little-endian field (pointers zero-extended from `ptr_width`), so
`*alen` = 8 × arity.

```c

# Mixed fat-object lock-out audit

Design audit for **bennett-4ht obligations 1–2**. No implementation or published contract is changed here. Build on the [shared scope](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md); that statement remains the common definition. The [08:45Z maintainer ruling][ruling] supplies the architectural constraint from [North Star §2][north-architecture], [§3.3][north-compilation] and [§4.4][north-ownership]. In particular, “whole-program” analysis must not become a requirement that the device object contain the whole native binary.

The significant lock-outs are the missing ordinary-pointer launch/memory contract (F04/F08/F23), mandatory transitive promotion of pointer-sharing host code (F14), and the unqualified inventory of every link global (F11). The remaining rows distinguish missing associations and missing tests from compatible device-only restrictions. Current ladder success would not establish mixed-binary execution: the gates inspect host bitcode but drive device objects directly.

## Evidence and severity

This is a read-only source audit of ISA 0.9, published dialect 0.2.12 and the 0.3 draft, the ladder's README/design/gate scripts, VM source interfaces and plugin documentation. The ladder and plugin still name older pinned contracts; their behavior is audited as written, without claiming that a 0.9 publication upgraded those consumers. No interpreter, build, corpus or full gate was run. VM findings are source observations, not reproduced runtime failures. Driver and runtimes obligations below derive from the published interfaces; their implementations were **not** inspected.

Locations below are file links with clause names and source line ranges. [sources.json](sources.json) records source SHA-256 values and the actual cited excerpts, since sibling worktrees are changing concurrently; line numbers describe that snapshot. [verify_audit.py](verify_audit.py) checks document structure, all 31 proposed beads, links and cited excerpts. It does not establish execution conformance.

- **locks out**: the present rule/interface cannot express the named mixed case. This identifies an exact amendment, not permission to reject the source program or discard the capability.
- **makes harder**: composition remains possible, but an association, integration interface or regression is missing. Absence of an end-to-end demonstration is not itself an architectural impossibility.
- **neutral**: a legitimate device-closure or explicit whole-device-mode rule. Preserve it and state its boundary; applying it to all native code would create a lock-out.

“Cheap now” prices the text/design decision or focused regression specification. It never claims that a native launch shim, shared-memory runtime, or full mixed rung is cheap to implement. Every row has one corresponding proposed bead in [cross-repo-beads.md](cross-repo-beads.md); neutral rows request a boundary clarification or preservation check, not removal of an invariant.

| F09 · bennett-isa-spec | [ISA §10 states][isa-launch], [§14.3 T4][isa-roundtrip] | Round trip restores the eligible **device run**, its active history and owned state; observed external events are not retracted, and measurement can make a run irreversible. | neutral | Add mixed-launch wording: reverse/close is per run, not rollback of native main, host libc, or all sibling device runs. Define the F08 memory writeback checkpoint and host-interference invalidation without weakening current ownership or measurement rules. | Yes: scope plus boundary protocol. |
| F10 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §§4.1–4.5][d2-tiers], [§19.1][d2-driver]; [0.3 same clauses][d3-tiers] [§19.1][d3-driver] | One merged LTO module is the analysis input; H originals and CC/R/L clones coexist. Calls without bitcode trap **in device variants** (§4.5). | neutral | Add: “Whole-program visibility computes device closures; it does not lower every input function. H-only functions and native-only dependencies outside those closures remain native and are not trapped merely for lacking device bitcode.” Preserve device taint, indirect candidate completeness, missing-plugin failure and polyvariance. | Yes: wording and a native-only dependency fixture. |
| F11 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §19.5][d2-layout], especially `globals: every global of the link`; [0.3 §§4.3, 17.1–17.2][d3-tiers] [memory][d3-memory] [§19.5][d3-layout] | Every link global can become device layout state; shared globals and address identity are stated as one memory. Read literally, unrelated native globals/TLS/libc state join the device inventory. | locks out | In a future revision define “complete device link” and its globals as device-reachable state plus boundary-visible shared state and required metadata. H-only state stays outside ISA layout/certificate inventory. Preserve one semantic identity for truly shared globals; do not clone or omit them. Prove closure selection sound for loads, indirect calls, allocation-validity and aliases. | Yes: scope definition now; closure/state analysis work later. |
| F12 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §19.2][d2-mode]; [0.3 §19.2][d3-mode], including argc/argv/envp, musl process context and exit handling | The **explicit whole-program reversible mode** makes main the device program and creates a CC argv stub; normal main return follows device exit/stdio cleanup. | neutral | Prefix the rule with its mode scope. Ordinary mixed mode retains native process startup, argv/envp, environ, libc context and exit handlers. Device functions receive declared arguments; an optional device process-context snapshot must be explicitly requested and marshalled with versioned argc/argv/envp lengths, byte/terminator encoding, error and lifetime rules. Returning from a marked function must return to native main, without running process-exit cleanup or fetching argv again. | Yes: exact mode discriminator and call/return design. |
| F13 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §§6.1, 6.4, 6.6][d2-handles] [boundary][d2-boundary]; [0.3 counterparts][d3-handles] [boundary][d3-boundary]; [North Star §3.1][north-handles] | Source handle operations and measurement-dependent device work cannot survive as native host execution. Existing boundary text narrowly permits analysed transport of opaque launch ids. | neutral | Preserve BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE/USE and the device-only qreg table. Clarify that launch-runtime opaque ids are not dereferenceable source pointers or permission to execute handle operations/measurement-derived control natively. Bind transport to F06, including target pointer width versus 64-bit ISA id and stale generations. Do not solve mixed execution by weakening escape checks. | Yes: terminology and positive/negative boundary tests. |
| F14 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §19.4][d2-placement]; [0.3 §19.4][d3-placement], [§25.3 ISA-11][d3-efficiency]; [ISA §0.3][isa-deferred] | Pointer-rich sharing **requires** H-to-CC promotion transitively to roots, potentially main. ISA-11 is described as only an efficiency/placement item. | locks out | Retain promotion as a valid current construction; add the F08 versioned native-sharing route and make promotion a selected realization, not a permanent prerequisite. State explicitly that promotion alone does not preserve the promised native host for this case. Record a mixed-boundary publication obligation distinct from the retired 0.9 lowering blockers; do not turn “no blocker active” into “mixed ABI complete.” | Yes: correct the architectural classification now; implementation deferred. |
| F15 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §8.5][d2-snapshot]; [0.3 §8.5][d3-snapshot], [§17.1][d3-memory] | Snapshot/replay reproduces caller allocations/bytes at device bump addresses; metadata and shadow-stack history are semantic dependencies, not just byte loads. Native malloc graphs do not automatically have that history/address model. | makes harder | Specify how F08 supplies equivalent bytes, pointer values/aliases, bounds/liveness, allocator and shadow-stack state before private allocations. Distinguish host-pointer registration from device-allocation replay; preserve private L entry-state semantics, snapshot invalidation and restoration. No blanket bit-copy of native pointers, silent relocation or mandatory promotion. | Yes: boundary algorithm/invariants now; implementation later. |
| F16 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §§4.4, 19.5][d2-tiers] [layout][d2-layout]; [0.3 §§4.4, 19.5][d3-tiers] [layout][d3-layout] | Comparison-link addresses are common across tiers; fixed non-PIE placement also constrains production host functions/globals that cross the boundary. Independent device layouts are not automatically one address space. | makes harder | Keep fixed shared-address layout as an admitted first profile; name it as such. In the mixed ABI publish container/root-to-layout association and mapping/relocation rules for other native layouts, including function-pointer dispatch and shared globals. Revalidate after device linking; do not change pointer comparisons/hashes or require native-only data/code in ISA memory. | Yes: profile and identities now; general relocation later. |
| F17 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §§4.5, 11.1–11.3][d2-tiers] [effects][d2-effects]; [0.3 counterparts][d3-tiers] [effects][d3-effects] | Device-reachable libc/libm/shims need bitcode and tier-specific effects; H calls its own libc. R has model effects; L is closed and virtualised. | neutral | State that libc closure is per device closure/variant, including all reachable helpers; host-only libc needs no device bitcode/certificate. Preserve fail-loud trapped device calls, R tape and L model state. Give a mixed example where native printf remains host libc and a marked R printf uses the reversible console model. | Yes: scope/example; no new hostcalls in R/L. |
| F18 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §11.4][d2-hostcalls]; [0.3 §11.4][d3-hostcalls] | CC-to-host calls use scalar and proved pointer-free copied pointees; pointer-rich arguments trigger promotion. This is an outbound service protocol, not a general inbound native-pointer launch binding. | makes harder | Document direction and alias/lifetime/copy/writeback limits. Add F08 as the future native-sharing path without changing current copies. Publish callback/re-entry rules, declaring when an existing run owns shared state and retaining genuine effect/reply ordering. Never infer host memory registration from a hostcall request buffer. | Yes: protocol boundaries now; shared callbacks later. |
| F19 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §9][d2-budgets]; [0.3 §§9.1–9.5][d3-budgets], especially staged capacities and launch tokens | Budget table/layout maxima cover shapes of a complete device link; tokens are minted per reference object/tuple, and replay/helper rows count. | makes harder | Scope table resolution and frozen capacities to the selected device link/program, retain per-root selection/final identities and per-call resolved tuples in container launch records. Host-only heap/stack/libc work has no device budget. Do not reset counters at shim boundaries, round tuples, or share a token solely because two objects have equal numeric budgets. | Yes: record/schema scoping. |
| F20 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §17.7][d2-comparison], [§19.7][d2-closed]; [0.3 counterparts][d3-comparison] [closed][d3-closed] | Native comparison code-generates materialised **device variants**, with matching runtime/layout and classifiers. Closed-unit mode checks closure and falls back to whole-program analysis. | neutral | State that the comparison subject is the selected materialised closure, not all of native main. Add a separate host integration comparison for mixed mode. Preserve original-input definedness evidence and device comparison support isolation. Closed-unit failure must fall back to analysis/linking, not force whole-binary lowering or reject the mixed host. | Yes: scope and regression specification. |
| F21 · bennett-driver | [0.2.12 §19.9 outputs][d2-output]; [0.3 §19.9][d3-output]; [North Star §4.4 ownership][north-ownership] | Outputs include native host module/stubs, ISA objects and sidecars, but no published container-to-entry-to-certificate inventory or full native call/return protocol. These are contract obligations, not a verified description of driver code. | makes harder | Publish the driver producer/consumer edge under the ISA-owned mixed-launch/container version: entry and ABI symbol, ISA object/linked closure/root, certificate/build/layout and invocation associations, versions, runtime ownership and load/unload. Keep plugin-defined filenames behind the adapter; do not canonise rung directory names as the ISA container format. | Yes: schema/design now; packaging/shim later. |
| F22 · bennett-vm | [src/launch.rs:245–304][vm-load]; [src/link.rs:43–80, 158–178][vm-link] | C loader parses one UTF-8 ISA object; source linker merges compatible device metadata and rejects unresolved imports. There is no native executable/container selector in this interface. | makes harder | Consume F01/F21 through a container adapter that extracts, verifies and selects linked ISA programs before calling the existing loader. Do not send ELF/native bytes to the ISA parser or merge all embedded objects merely because they share a host.
