| F31 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §§4.2, 7.6, 19.1][d2-tiers] [outlining][d2-outlining] [pipeline][d2-driver]; [0.3 counterparts][d3-tiers] [outlining][d3-outlining] [pipeline][d3-driver] | Tier assignment sees functions after post-link optimisation; a function containing markers is CC. Region bodies are outlined, but a surrounding native-to-device entry wrapper has no stated mixed-boundary preservation rule. Inference: legal wrapper inlining could pull markers into main and expand the device partition. | makes harder | Specify how mixed mode preserves or reconstructs the native/device entry boundary across LTO: a supported standard LLVM noinline/build boundary or sound post-optimisation re-outlining, with declared ABI and identities. Keep the allowed LLVM pipeline and no-pre-link-plugin rule. Add a wrapper-small-enough-to-inline fixture proving main stays native; do not claim an observed compiler failure from this text-only audit. | Yes: define boundary/pipeline contract now; compiler realization later. |

## ISA spec

F01–F09 leave the inner ISA closed and independently verifiable. Native container fields belong to a separate versioned envelope, not ad hoc fields that current §9.1 must reject. Two embedded objects need not be flattened into one conflicting layout. If they actually import each other, §9.3 resolution, whole device-closure validation and certificate rebinding still apply.

The certificate change is a scope statement plus an association, not a demand to create region records for native functions or every CC/R function. All records that the selected device closure actually requires remain mandatory. Separate device roots can reuse static callee code while retaining distinct invocation bindings and root-relative contexts. [Current §11 action-support text][isa-support] already states the desired native/device boundary for `$construct`/`$release`; generalise that good example to §§9/12/13 rather than claiming the entire published spec excludes fat objects.

The real missing capability is native-memory binding. §10 copies argument buffers, while §7 describes device-address memory. Neither establishes a valid live host pointer. Reversal must also specify when host-visible writes commit, how the host excludes interference, and which version is restored. Those mechanics cannot be replaced by “promote main” while claiming to retain an ordinary host.

## Dialect spec

F10–F20 and F31 apply to both the published 0.2.12 text and the 0.3 draft; the latter changes handle/budget/R realization, not the existence of a native-container ABI. Amend a future version through the contract process; do not silently rewrite the published compatibility unit. In 0.3, “no blocker is active on ISA 0.9” ([§25.1][d3-blockers]) retires named device lowering blockers. It is not evidence that native pointer sharing or mixed packaging is already specified.

Whole-program LTO, polyvariant cloning and complete device closure are compatible with selective lowering. The problem is universalising “every global of the link,” transitive promotion, and whole-device main startup. Likewise, “handles never in host native code” protects source-level device operations. Existing opaque launch-id transport does not allow native dereference, arithmetic on handle identity, gate execution, or moving measurement-dependent device control to H. The first mixed fixture can leave all such values inside device/runtime boundaries; later persistence tests must preserve those rules.

Device libc closure and the materialised native comparator must remain strict. A host-only libc or native library does not need an ISA record. Sharing a source function between H and L still requires a device version and shared-state semantics; labelling a callee “host-only” cannot hide a device-reachable call.

F31 is an inference about the interaction of post-link optimisation and function-granularity tier assignment, not a reproduced inliner bug. Source module boundaries alone do not promise that a small marked wrapper survives LTO. The mixed profile must state a supported boundary-preservation or re-outlining construction rather than relying on that accident.

## VM

F22–F24 follow from executable interface bodies, not the launch module's stale “0.8” header comment. The current program object owns one device Object and Resources; the loader takes UTF-8 ISA text. This is suitable as an inner loader beneath a fat-object adapter. Its per-program locks and per-run journals are useful boundaries to retain.

The C argument matcher has no ordinary-pointer binding, and the sparse device memory backend is not native host memory. `--mem global=hex` and CLI integer-pointer values do not remedy that. Add memory and container routing only against published contracts. No claim is made that running the VM CLI today launches a native host or that saved-array replay validates container identity.

## Plugin

F25 preserves the plan's H-original/device-clone split. Documentation contains older milestone and fixture descriptions; [the Resume block][plugin-resume] reports later work, so an old “unimplemented” sentence is not treated as a reproduced current production failure. The audit uses those documents to identify the interface obligations still needing mixed acceptance, without reading or modifying plugin implementation.

The key regression will combine native-only work and a real marked module, verify that only the latter's reachable device closure appears in ISA objects, then execute the generated host stub. Scalar reference objects and a native mock protocol do not establish that integration.

## Driver and runtimes (contract consumers)

F21 is a driver publication task inferred from dialect §19.9 and North Star §4.4, not a source-level driver defect. ISA owns the exchanged format; the driver owns producing/consuming it. Container layout, shim ABI, root selection, versions and lifetime rules must be published before cross-repo implementation, rather than copied from another repository's source or inferred from `out/main` filenames.

Runtime implementation follow-ups belong under the F08/F12/F17/F18 contracts: native startup/libc belongs to native main, device process context only to a requested whole-device mode, and R/L effect models remain distinct from host I/O. No new runtime implementation bead is proposed independently of those decisions; the orchestrator should attach owning implementation work when the versioned contract is accepted.

## Conformance ladder

F26–F30 identify a coverage gap, not a reason to weaken existing acceptance. Rung 3 deliberately demonstrates an entire reversible musl program; rung 4 deliberately requires the real SQLite device closure and fixed database domain. Their strict libc, tape, domain, shape and evidence-label checks stay intact. Rungs 1/2 also retain their current domains and reversal checks. Rungs 5–8 have acceptance READMEs rather than an inspected mixed implementation gate; no undocumented mixed acceptance is inferred from them.

The first amendment is an **additional** mixed execution test with its own status and evidence. File F30 when rung 3 or 4 lands, as requested by bennett-4ht; it is not a new prerequisite to either rung. Lack of a published mixed ABI must appear as an explicit dependency, never a reason to reinterpret a passing whole-device rung as a passing mixed rung. The proposed first scalar fixture witnesses only the native/device split; pointer-rich memory sharing, cross-launch resources and larger applications remain explicit obligations.

## Verification and handoff

Run the focused documentation check (256 MiB address-space limit inside the checker, 30-second outer limit; no full gates):

```sh
timeout 30s env -u DISPLAY -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY -u DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS -u BROWSER TMPDIR=/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/.tmp/sol-4ht-audit/tmp python3 /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-4ht-audit/verify_audit.py
```

The expected result is structural/citation validation only. All proposed beads carry label `fat-object`; none has been filed by this job. The runner owns commits and the orchestrator owns cross-repo filing and accepted publication changes. No git command, remote sync or implementation gate is part of this audit.

### 3.3 Types, ownership, operand schema

```
  iN (1 ≤ N ≤ 128)   ptr (width ptr_width)   f64   token   qreg[n]   qreg[*]   region[A,W]
  wire   wire[N]      linear values in structured code and interfaces
  reg[N]              a location (oracle bodies, lprocs); not linear
```

**Widths.** The bound 1 ≤ N ≤ 128 applies to the type `iN` only (a lifted
row's `.iN` suffix names a location width, §4.2). `wire[N]`,
`reg[N]` and `qreg[n]` have N, n ≥ 1 and `region[A,W]` has A ≥ 0, W ≥ 1,
with no upper bound (a `qreg[*]` register's width is a run-time value n
≥ 0, §3.3 below); a value of these types is a bit-vector of its
width in every semantics (§14.1), including interface bundles,
pseudo-locations, locations, cells, stack cells and frame slots.

**Linear SSA with implicit versioning** (structured code and CC region
calls): a `wire`/`wire[N]` value is produced once and consumed once;
mode `in` consumes and re-produces a successor of the same name;
`consume` consumes; `produce` creates; a name is not used after
`consume`. **Regions are threaded the same way.** **Indexed operand**
`w[i]` (literal i) borrows wire i for one instruction.

**Disjointness.** The wire operands of one instruction are pairwise
distinct; **every wire of the effective control context is distinct from
every target and from every consumed or produced operand** of an
instruction executed under it (so `qpred p; x p` is ill-formed); a
predicate wire is not modified between its push and its matching pop; a
`qif`/`qloop … while` selector is not modified inside its arms or body.
A region's interface bundles and regions are pairwise disjoint.
Distinctness is judged on the wires denoted: a bundle and an indexed
operand of it, or two indexed operands denoting the same wire, overlap
and are not distinct. [deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.4]

**Per-opcode operand schema** (§4.8): every opcode has a tier set, a
fixed arity, operand kinds and a result type; results are written first
(`w = op a b`), modes are never written, and **result types that are not
determined by the operands are written as a suffix** (`r = const.i32 5`,
`r = zext.i64 a`, `r = trunc.i8 a`, `r = ptrtoint.i64 p`, `p = inttoptr
a`). Width rules: `binop`: `iN` operands of equal width; `icmp`: `iN` or
`ptr` operands of equal width and the same type (CC/R; lifted forms:
§4.2); `select` arms equal width; `zext`/`sext` strictly widen, `trunc`
strictly narrows; `load.iN`
and `lift.load.iN` require N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}; `measure` of `qreg[n]`
with n ≤ 128 yields `in`, and `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) of a `qreg[*]`
yields `iN`; signed division truncates toward zero. The
## 4. Instruction set

### 4.1 Classical control (CC, R)

| Instruction | Form | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| `const` | `r = const.iN c` | |
| `add sub mul and or xor shl lshr ashr` | `r = op a b` | wrap; shift ≥ N → 0 |
| `udiv urem sdiv srem` | `r = op a b` | divisor 0 or signed MIN/−1: CC trap; R fault record (`FAULT_UB_DIV0` / `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW`, §4.9) |
| `icmp` | `r = icmp.{eq,ne,ult,ule,slt,sle} a b` | |
| `select` | `r = select p a b` | |
| `zext sext trunc` | `r = op.iM a` | |
| `ptrtoint` / `inttoptr` | `r = ptrtoint.iM p` / `p = inttoptr a` | zero-extend/truncate to M / to ptr_width |
| `load` / `store` | `r = load.iN addr` / `store.iN addr v` | N/8 bytes little-endian, unaligned; outside every live allocation: CC trap / R fault record. The suffix is always `.iN` with N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}: there is no pointer-typed load or store (a pointer is stored as `ptrtoint.iM` with M = ptr_width and reloaded with `inttoptr`) |
| `alloc` / `free` | `p = alloc n` / `free p` | §7.1 |
| `br` | `br ^b(args)` / `br p ^b1(args) ^b2(args)` | parallel block-parameter assignment |
| `call` / `ret` | `results = call @f args` / `ret vals` | arguments and values are bare operands (`r = call @f a b`, `(r1, r2) = call @f a`, `call @f`, `ret v`, `ret`); results bind in order; a parenthesised argument list is ill-formed for `call`; callee tiers per §3.1 |
| `uncall` | `uncall @f (args) (results)` | **segment uncall**, legal exactly under §7.4: the most recent completed call segment of the history is a call of `@f` whose recorded boundary argument and result values equal the current operand values, every frame after it is pure, and it contains, at any nesting depth, no `measure` frame and no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified; otherwise CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP` with no state change beyond the fault record. The segment is reversed by the cancellation-aware history traversal of §7.4 (LIFO, nested segments included; frames already reversed and `uncall` markers skipped) and its frames are marked reversed; the `uncall` itself pushes a frame naming the segment |
| `trap` | `trap` | CC trap; R fault record (`FAULT_UB_TRAP`, §4.9) |
| `fault` | `fault C` | CC and R; literal 0 ≤ C < 2^64; one terminal record with code C for C ≠ 0, else 4 (§5.4); run ends `FAILED`, no successor executes, prefix history retained for reversal (§7.4, §10, §14.1). One classical instruction (§8.2); in an oracle body or lproc, the one-budget-step §4.2 row instead |
| `qreg.new qreg.free qreg.slice qreg.concat qreg.width qreg.id qreg.from_id` | §3.3 | CC and R (§3.3) |
| `measure` | `r = measure q` / `r = measure.iN q` | CC only; Born rule (§14.2); consumes q; `.iN` for a `qreg[*]` (§3.3) |
| `budget.mint` | `tk = budget.mint @o s h st d` | CC and R; a token for the tuple (s, h, st, d) validated for oracle `@o` (§5.4) |
| `effect` | `effect dev.op args` | R only (§7.3); results are written to memory operands |
| `hostcall` | `rlen = hostcall service ptr len` | CC only (§10) |

**Measurement draws.** Supplied draws are consumed FIFO, one per
executed measurement in dynamic execution order (including measurements
of deterministic states); a failing precondition consumes no draw.
Each draw is in [0,1). Enumerate outcomes lexicographically by their
printed bits string, wire 0 first, and select the first outcome whose
cumulative Born weight is **strictly greater** than the draw times the
total weight. Thus an exact CDF tie selects the next nonzero-weight
outcome. Returned integers remain little-endian (wire 0 is bit 0).
This fixes the reference CLI convention; unsupplied draws follow the
Born distribution (§14.2).

**DRAW-ORDER witness**

```json
{"draws":["0.25","0.75"],"integer_order_values":[1,3],"outcomes_bits":["01","11"],"outcomes_values":[2,3],"reversed_draw_values":[3,2]}
```

Prepare two fresh two-wire registers with H on both wires and measure
them in order. Each has weights 1/4 on `00`, `01`, `10`, `11` in that
order. The first draw selects `01` (integer 2) and the second `11`
(integer 3). Reversed draw consumption would give integers 3,2;
integer-ordered outcomes would give 1,3. Both draws test exact CDF ties.

### 4.2 Lifted rows (oracle bodies and lprocs)
## 9. Objects, identity, linking, reversible ABI (object 0.9)

### 9.1 Sections and schemas

Order fixed: `symbols imports functions data devices layout schedule
certificate coverage`; each at most once; unknown sections or fields are
errors. Metadata sections are one JCS line each, an object:

```
  symbols:  { "<local index>": { "name", "kind": "cc"|"r"|"oracle"|"action"|"perm_action"|"lproc"|"data", "signature": <sig>, "interface_hash" } }
  imports:  { "<name>": { "signature": <sig>, "pinned_closure_hash"?: hex } }
  devices:  { "models": { "<dev>": { "version": "0.9" } }, "services": { "<reverse-dns id>": { "version": "…" } } }
  layout:   { "ptr_width", "H", "A_tab", "S_cells", "null_size", "heap_base", "heap_limit_max",
              "globals": { "<name>": { "base", "size", "image": "<data name>" } },
              "interface_regions": { "<oracle>": { "<region>": { "base" } } }, "block_image"?: "<data name>" }
  schedule: §11      certificate: §12      coverage: { "entries": [ §13 ] }
  <sig>:    kind "oracle": { "tier":"q", "kind", "in":[w…], "tgt":w, "fault":F, "mem":[{"A","W","role"}], "args":[types], "abi":"0.9" }
            kind "action"/"perm_action": { "tier":"q", "kind", "mut":[w…], "in":[w…], "mem":[…], "args":[types], "bounds":[…], "abi" }
            kind "lproc": { "tier":"q", "kind", "params":[w…], "result":w, "tokens":["token"…], "args":[types], "mem":[{"A","W","role":"in"}], "abi" }
            kind "cc"/"r": { "tier", "kind", "params":[types], "results":[types], "abi" }
            kind "data": { "tier":"-", "kind", "size", "abi" }
```

Integers that may exceed 2^53 are decimal strings, and a JSON number ≥
2^53 anywhere in metadata is an error. `data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`.
**Encodings.** `types` in `args`, `params` and `results` are the §16.2
type spellings as JSON strings (`"i64"`, `"ptr"`, `"f64"`, `"token"`,
`"qreg[8]"`); `w` in `in`, `tgt`, `mut`, lproc `params` and `result` is
the integer bundle or location width (§16.3). An lproc's `tokens`,
`args` and `mem` arrays are mandatory (empty arrays when absent from the
header); `args` excludes tokens and `mem` has only `in` roles (§3.7).
Array order is header order within each group. A **program-point id**
(§9.2) is the JSON array `[interface_hash, block name, ordinal]`.
Certificate `frame` fields use exactly the typed-record schema of §9.4;
unknown fields are errors there as in every metadata record.
`devices.models` lists every §7.3 model that an `effect` of the object
uses (an `effect` on an unlisted §7.3 model is a load error; a device
outside §7.3 is never listed and faults at run time, §17 item 3; a key
that is not a §7.3 model is an unknown field); `devices.services` lists
every `hostcall` service (§10). An interface region's name (its param
name and its `layout.interface_regions.<oracle>` key) MUST differ from
every symbol and import name of the object, since `@name` in `lift.addr`
denotes either (§9.2).

### 9.2 Identity

An lproc's token, classical and region parameter groups are part of
its JCS signature (§9.1), including their order and types. The header
and every forwarded operand list are canonical function bytes; changing
them changes the envelope and, through references, the caller's closure
hash. Fixed binding instances are derived per oracle (§3.7), not new
symbols or separately hashed bodies; instance rows share the source
row's program-point id and expand once per fixed row instance per step,
while the instance and semantic-call occurrence components in §11
invocation paths and §12.1 context keys distinguish their uses. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11] Entry argument values and token tuples bind invocations through
`params_hash`, not through the lproc's interface hash (this section).
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
the key under `globals` to the global name.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-9.1-P1]

**Envelope boundary lemma** (non-normative).
For a function symbol, §16.2 requires a header beginning `fn `,
`oracle `, `action `, `perm_action ` or `lproc `; following lines are
`regs`, block labels, instructions, or structured closing braces. [deps: ISA-16.2]
No §4 opcode is `data`, `layout` or `device`; a result with one of
those names has ` = ` after the name, which cannot begin any part-(5)
line (a data name, a JSON array, or a device name is required there). [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
No canonical function line is a bare 64-hex string, so the first
part-(5)/(6) line ends the function bytes unambiguously. [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-16.2]
The fixed `data `, `layout `, `device ` prefixes distinguish (5a–c),
and a bare 64-hex line distinguishes (6); JSON strings escape LF. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
For a data symbol, the signature kind selects exactly one data line
for (4), with (5)–(6) empty, even when the data name itself is `data`,
`layout` or `device`. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
Together with the fixed first three lines and the injective JCS array
paths, these facts make the envelope parts recoverable without length
prefixes; this is an encoding argument, not a claim of SHA-256 injectivity. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1, ISA-16.2, ISA-9.2-P4]

### 9.3 Linking

For mixed native/device containers, the selected device-object/root inventory, image preparation, native-model, resource, frame, borrow-proof and token-alias bindings follow §12.3's mixed fat-object scope and attachment rules (K85–K91). The native container does not replace the selected ISA object identity.

Imports resolve by name + signature (mismatch: link error; pinned hash:
equality required); resolved closure hashes are recorded in the linked
certificate. Equal-closure-hash duplicates deduplicate; unequal under one
name, or conflicting `devices`/`layout`/`data` entries: link error.
Linking = resolution, validation, certificate composition (§12.3),
rebinding to closure hashes.

### 9.4 Reversible ABI

# F15/F16 — native entry import and address profiles

Beads: **b-dialect-spec-5dl**, **b-dialect-spec-dce**. One memory-profile amendment covering import and observations. Amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4; F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands.

## Exact defect and clause citations

[Dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) and [0.3](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md) §8.5 reconstruct caller bytes and allocation/shadow-stack history at device bump addresses. §§4.4/17.1–17.3/19.5 tie pointer observations to a fixed comparison link and one memory identity. Native malloc graphs do not have the required bump history automatically; independent embedded programs do not become one address space. [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F15/F16 identify missing association/import mechanics, not a failure of existing replay.

## North Star gap

Boundary mechanics for North Star §§3.2–3.3/4.4, feeding the genuine native-memory gap in F08. Existing snapshot/replay remains valid; it cannot be the only eventual means of live native sharing. Pointer equality, ptrtoint/address hashing, function dispatch, liveness and allocator behavior remain part of defined observations.

## Alternatives and witnesses

Native nodes at 0x10000 and 0x18000 form a cycle with two aliases to the second node's interior value. A size-only bump replay at device heap 0x2000 produces different addresses and pointer-derived results. Blind byte copying leaves native pointer words unmapped. A registered same-address profile preserves those observations where layouts/ranges agree; a different mapping needs a published observation-preserving representation, including integer-derived pointers and dispatch. A native free/reuse can change liveness/generation without changing loaded bytes, so byte-only snapshot caching is unsound. [verify.py](verify.py) checks aliases and metadata/epoch invalidation, not an actual native-memory backend.

## Recommended exact dialect diff

Append to §19.5 in both future tracks:

```text
The existing fixed non-PIE shared-address comparison/production construction
is profile fixed-shared-address-1. Retain all range/alignment/nonoverlap
checks and common addresses for values crossing the boundary. Bind each
selected program/root to its concrete layout, build record and memory
profile; co-packaging distinct layouts does not merge them.

Other native layouts require an explicit versioned mapping/relocation
profile covering globals, data/function pointers, indirect dispatch and
defined pointer comparisons/ptrtoint/address hashes. The profile preserves
those source observations; it cannot silently relocate unidentified
integer words or require native-only code/data in ISA memory. Revalidate
the complete linked device layout and profile after linking/rebinding.
```

Append to §8.5 and cross-reference §§17.1–17.3:

```text
Device-allocation replay and native registration are different entry-state
sources. For a registered native graph, acquire a consistent entry epoch
under the memory profile before reading bytes or metadata. Compute the
complete byte and metadata dependency closure (§19.5), including aliases,
cyclic/interior pointers, allocation-validity/address observations and
logical shadow-stack dependencies even when the byte read set is empty.

Canonicalize allocation identities without merging distinct live objects;
map all pointer observations by the selected profile. Import bytes,
bounds/liveness, pinned/ownership state and required allocator/shadow-stack
metadata before any private oracle allocation/access. Do not require native
malloc to have followed the device bump history or infer live logical
frames from backing-storage containment. The private construction must
produce the same defined entry observations, allocation/fault outcomes and
addresses as the corresponding materialised native closure. Any host-
compatible allocator/escaping-storage profile is explicit, not an unchecked
conversion of a private device address to a native pointer.

Retain §8.5's private L memory semantics: writes are restored/discarded
except declared outputs, input regions remain unchanged, and no native
load at a coherent address is introduced. Device bump replay keeps its
existing allocation order, invalidation, register unpreparation and charges.
An alternative native import needs published exact metadata/address and
allocation rules; these are required mechanics, not permission to promote
all native callers or narrow their allocation graphs.

Snapshot reuse requires identical dependent bytes AND allocator/liveness/
generation/shadow-stack metadata and entry mapping epoch. Relevant native
free/realloc, logical stack changes or rebinding invalidate it even if the
byte read set is unchanged. New host edits between closed launches are
part of the next entry epoch. Export/return/close obey the memory profile;
private L state is not blindly written back as shared host mutation.
```

The exact arbitrary-layout/allocator wire realization remains a versioned publication obligation under [native-sharing](native-sharing.md); the fixed profile is an admitted first construction, not the final capability boundary.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Fixed source images/layouts affect object/closure/build hashes; runtime registration epochs/mappings bind invocations and private entry-state identities. Metadata changes can invalidate cached snapshots without changing source bytes. Container/root/layout identities must agree with certificates and comparators. No ref recipe edit is inherent; if the accepted import adds ordinary materialised helper/replay rows, their bodies and costs become part of the actual device/reference closure. Driver/plugin/runtime/VM/checkers pin that published realization.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Fixed-address compatibility; noncontiguous cyclic native allocations; interior aliases and address-sensitive outputs; function pointers; shared globals; incompatible pointer width/layout; colliding ranges; metadata-only invalidation; repeated host edits; private L restoration; stack backing versus logical extents; escaping allocation and native free/realloc. Compare from the same entry identity/address configuration rather than relocated independent baselines. [INDEX](INDEX.md) records finite alias/epoch checks; no pointer relocation or allocator implementation is claimed.

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

Next versioned dialect revision plus 0.3 draft; default companion/**0.10** memory semantics. ISA 0.9's layout/bump model cannot silently acquire arbitrary native allocations. An in-version K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including coherent import and allocation semantics. Suggested decision `MIXED-ENTRY-MEMORY-PROFILES`; no unversioned catalog change.

## Mixed fat object

Use [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F15/F16. Each marked device program/root has a concrete layout/certificate/context `[]`, budgets/ledger and run/tape, while native main/argv/envp/libc and unrelated state stay native. Explicit mappings and allocator obligations preserve live native sharing rather than replacing it with universal promotion. These amendments never gate rungs 3/4; F30 stays deferred.
# F18 — copied outbound services and registered inbound calls

Bead: **b-dialect-spec-3ca**. Amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4; F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands.

## Exact defect and clause citations

[Dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) and [0.3](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md) §11.4 describe CC-to-H scalar/pointer-free copied service arguments and writeback, with pointer-rich callees promoted. [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md) §§7.4/10 retain request/reply history and validate a copied live service range. Neither is an inbound native-pointer registration or a callback/reentry ownership contract. §11.4 also lacks a complete alias policy for overlapping copied pointees. See [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F18.

## North Star gap

Direction/ownership publication, not authority to infer a live pointer from a service byte block. Native-sharing capability remains required under F04/F08 for North Star §§3.3/4.4. The existing copied CC path and R/L effect restrictions remain valid within their domain.

## Alternatives and witnesses

Device CC sends a four-byte pointer-free pointee to an H service. The host stub writes 7 to its copy; only the declared reply/writeback updates device memory. This grants no live access to the caller's arbitrary native graph. Two overlapping pointee views must share one copied backing range if the service can observe aliasing; independent copies can return the wrong result. A callback into a second run touching the first run's still-leased range conflicts even while the first is HOST_REQUEST. Prefer copied-range alias preservation plus an explicit future registered/reentry profile, rather than silently releasing ownership during host service.

## Recommended exact dialect diff

Append to §11.4:

```text
This is an outbound CC service protocol. Its pointer-free proof, explicit
extent, scalar encoding, copy ownership and reply/writeback bounds do not
register native storage or implement inbound native pointer arguments.
The service stub may use only its copied ranges for that request; request
payload lifetime is not a general host allocation lifetime. Preserve full
reply length/truncation and before-mutation range checks of ISA §10.

For pointer-free aliased arguments, normalize overlapping views of one
device allocation into a common copied backing range, preserve their
relative offsets and alias observations, and write back each covered byte
once under a specified consistent reply. The versioned service-block
profile records that range/view map; it does not infer aliasing from two
independent byte arrays. Independent/disjoint copies remain valid when
the analysis proves their observations equivalent. Native code must not
retain a service-copy pointer after reply. Pointer-rich native sharing
uses the separately published registration profile, not silent promotion
as the only eventual realization (§19.4).

HOST_REQUEST does not close the run or release its device/registered-memory
ownership. A service callback/reentry profile records parent run/request,
program/root, ordering and exact permitted leases. Reentry with disjoint
state may use an independent run under existing ownership rules. Conflicting
shared state requires a published atomic ownership delegation/handoff:
quiesce the parent, record exact ranges/generations and owner chain, prevent
parent access while delegated, restore ownership on reply/failure, and bind
all child history/results before resuming. Without that protocol the
conflict is diagnosed before mutation, not bypassed by a hidden unlock.
This names required future mechanics, not a source-level callback ban.

Retain R effect/CC reply/native event separation and source order. Recovery
uses retained replies without reissuing the service or callback. Reversing
the device buffer/history does not retract external host effects. R/L
remain closed to genuine hostcalls; registration does not change their
tier rules or permit native handle operations.
```

The new alias-range service encoding and any shared callback handoff need a versioned publication before consumers emit them. No new numeric service tag or current C API is introduced by this proposal.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Service declaration/adapter/body changes can change device object/closure/build hashes. Per-request copied bytes, range maps, replies and parent/child ownership bind invocation/journal records. They are not native pointer identities. No ref catalog change follows from documenting direction. Driver/plugin/runtime/VM/replay consumers must pin a published service/profile encoding rather than share implementation code.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Require pointer-free copy/writeback/truncation; aliasing overlapping views versus disjoint ranges; no retained pointer; bad range/reply before mutation; pointer-rich path using accepted registration; disjoint reentry; conflicting lease rejected before mutation; future handoff success/failure ownership restoration; exactly-once retained replies; R/L no genuine hostcalls. The finite model in [verify.py](verify.py) checks alias and lease counterexamples only; actual host-service execution is not claimed in [INDEX](INDEX.md).

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

Next maintained dialect revision plus 0.3 draft; new service/registration/callback profiles default to companion/**0.10** integration. Existing ISA copied hostcall semantics are preserved. An in-version 0.9 K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including service-block alias encoding and old consumer negotiation. Proposed decision `MIXED-HOSTCALL-DIRECTION-AND-REENTRY`.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F18. Native main/libc remain native; only marked device closures receive certificates/context `[]`, budgets and ledger ownership. Requests/journals bind their selected device run; native-memory sharing requires explicit registration, not whole-binary promotion. These amendments never gate rungs 3/4; F30 stays deferred.

# Mixed fat-object lock-out audit

Design audit for **bennett-4ht obligations 1–2**. No implementation or published contract is changed here. Build on the [shared scope](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md); that statement remains the common definition. The [08:45Z maintainer ruling][ruling] supplies the architectural constraint from [North Star §2][north-architecture], [§3.3][north-compilation] and [§4.4][north-ownership]. In particular, “whole-program” analysis must not become a requirement that the device object contain the whole native binary.

The significant lock-outs are the missing ordinary-pointer launch/memory contract (F04/F08/F23), mandatory transitive promotion of pointer-sharing host code (F14), and the unqualified inventory of every link global (F11). The remaining rows distinguish missing associations and missing tests from compatible device-only restrictions. Current ladder success would not establish mixed-binary execution: the gates inspect host bitcode but drive device objects directly.

## Evidence and severity

This is a read-only source audit of ISA 0.9, published dialect 0.2.12 and the 0.3 draft, the ladder's README/design/gate scripts, VM source interfaces and plugin documentation. The ladder and plugin still name older pinned contracts; their behavior is audited as written, without claiming that a 0.9 publication upgraded those consumers. No interpreter, build, corpus or full gate was run. VM findings are source observations, not reproduced runtime failures. Driver and runtimes obligations below derive from the published interfaces; their implementations were **not** inspected.

Locations below are file links with clause names and source line ranges. [sources.json](sources.json) records source SHA-256 values and the actual cited excerpts, since sibling worktrees are changing concurrently; line numbers describe that snapshot. [verify_audit.py](verify_audit.py) checks document structure, all 31 proposed beads, links and cited excerpts. It does not establish execution conformance.

- **locks out**: the present rule/interface cannot express the named mixed case. This identifies an exact amendment, not permission to reject the source program or discard the capability.
- **makes harder**: composition remains possible, but an association, integration interface or regression is missing. Absence of an end-to-end demonstration is not itself an architectural impossibility.
- **neutral**: a legitimate device-closure or explicit whole-device-mode rule. Preserve it and state its boundary; applying it to all native code would create a lock-out.

“Cheap now” prices the text/design decision or focused regression specification. It never claims that a native launch shim, shared-memory runtime, or full mixed rung is cheap to implement. Every row has one corresponding proposed bead in [cross-repo-beads.md](cross-repo-beads.md); neutral rows request a boundary clarification or preservation check, not removal of an invariant.

## ISA spec

F01–F09 leave the inner ISA closed and independently verifiable. Native container fields belong to a separate versioned envelope, not ad hoc fields that current §9.1 must reject. Two embedded objects need not be flattened into one conflicting layout. If they actually import each other, §9.3 resolution, whole device-closure validation and certificate rebinding still apply.

The certificate change is a scope statement plus an association, not a demand to create region records for native functions or every CC/R function. All records that the selected device closure actually requires remain mandatory. Separate device roots can reuse static callee code while retaining distinct invocation bindings and root-relative contexts. [Current §11 action-support text][isa-support] already states the desired native/device boundary for `$construct`/`$release`; generalise that good example to §§9/12/13 rather than claiming the entire published spec excludes fat objects.

The real missing capability is native-memory binding. §10 copies argument buffers, while §7 describes device-address memory. Neither establishes a valid live host pointer. Reversal must also specify when host-visible writes commit, how the host excludes interference, and which version is restored. Those mechanics cannot be replaced by “promote main” while claiming to retain an ordinary host.

## Dialect spec

F10–F20 and F31 apply to both the published 0.2.12 text and the 0.3 draft; the latter changes handle/budget/R realization, not the existence of a native-container ABI. Amend a future version through the contract process; do not silently rewrite the published compatibility unit. In 0.3, “no blocker is active on ISA 0.9” ([§25.1][d3-blockers]) retires named device lowering blockers. It is not evidence that native pointer sharing or mixed packaging is already specified.

Whole-program LTO, polyvariant cloning and complete device closure are compatible with selective lowering. The problem is universalising “every global of the link,” transitive promotion, and whole-device main startup. Likewise, “handles never in host native code” protects source-level device operations. Existing opaque launch-id transport does not allow native dereference, arithmetic on handle identity, gate execution, or moving measurement-dependent device control to H. The first mixed fixture can leave all such values inside device/runtime boundaries; later persistence tests must preserve those rules.

Device libc closure and the materialised native comparator must remain strict. A host-only libc or native library does not need an ISA record. Sharing a source function between H and L still requires a device version and shared-state semantics; labelling a callee “host-only” cannot hide a device-reachable call.

F31 is an inference about the interaction of post-link optimisation and function-granularity tier assignment, not a reproduced inliner bug. Source module boundaries alone do not promise that a small marked wrapper survives LTO. The mixed profile must state a supported boundary-preservation or re-outlining construction rather than relying on that accident.

## VM

F22–F24 follow from executable interface bodies, not the launch module's stale “0.8” header comment. The current program object owns one device Object and Resources; the loader takes UTF-8 ISA text. This is suitable as an inner loader beneath a fat-object adapter. Its per-program locks and per-run journals are useful boundaries to retain.

The C argument matcher has no ordinary-pointer binding, and the sparse device memory backend is not native host memory. `--mem global=hex` and CLI integer-pointer values do not remedy that. Add memory and container routing only against published contracts. No claim is made that running the VM CLI today launches a native host or that saved-array replay validates container identity.

## Plugin

F25 preserves the plan's H-original/device-clone split. Documentation contains older milestone and fixture descriptions; [the Resume block][plugin-resume] reports later work, so an old “unimplemented” sentence is not treated as a reproduced current production failure. The audit uses those documents to identify the interface obligations still needing mixed acceptance, without reading or modifying plugin implementation.

def memory_and_ownership():
    storage = bytearray([10, 20, 30, 40])
    storage[1] += 3
    assert list(storage) == [10, 23, 30, 40]
    storage[0] = 99
    entry = bytes(storage)
    storage[0] ^= 1
    assert storage[0] == 98
    storage[:] = entry
    assert list(storage) == [99, 23, 30, 40]
    nodes = {'A': {'next': 'B', 'value': 2}, 'B': {'next': 'A', 'value': 5}}
    first = ('B', 'value')
    second = (nodes['A']['next'], 'value')
    assert first == second and nodes[nodes['B']['next']]['next'] == 'B'
    nodes[first[0]][first[1]] = 7
    assert nodes[second[0]][second[1]] == 7
    copies = [{'value': 5}, {'value': 5}]
    copies[0]['value'] = 7
    assert copies[1]['value'] != 7  # independent copies are a counterexample
    snapshot_key = (bytes(storage), ('alloc', 1, True), ('stack', 4), 9)
    assert snapshot_key != (bytes(storage), ('alloc', 2, True), ('stack', 4), 9)
    assert snapshot_key != (bytes(storage), ('alloc', 1, True), ('stack', 5), 9)
    assert snapshot_key != (bytes(storage), ('alloc', 1, True), ('stack', 4), 10)
    owner = ('P', 1, 'qreg', 7)
    assert owner != ('Q', 1, 'qreg', 7) and owner != ('P', 2, 'qreg', 7)
    assert (1 << 40) != ((1 << 40) & ((1 << 32) - 1))
    lease = {'owner': ('P', 'run1'), 'range': (0, 4), 'open': True}
    conflict = lambda lo, hi: lease['open'] and lo < 4 and hi > 0
    assert conflict(1, 2) and not conflict(4, 8)

    assert conflict(0, 4)
    lease['open'] = False
    assert not conflict(0, 4)

def closure_inventory_and_modes():
# F15/F16 — native entry import and address profiles

Beads: **b-dialect-spec-5dl**, **b-dialect-spec-dce**. One memory-profile amendment covering import and observations. Amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4; F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands.

## Exact defect and clause citations

[Dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) and [0.3](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md) §8.5 reconstruct caller bytes and allocation/shadow-stack history at device bump addresses. §§4.4/17.1–17.3/19.5 tie pointer observations to a fixed comparison link and one memory identity. Native malloc graphs do not have the required bump history automatically; independent embedded programs do not become one address space. [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F15/F16 identify missing association/import mechanics, not a failure of existing replay.

## North Star gap

Boundary mechanics for North Star §§3.2–3.3/4.4, feeding the genuine native-memory gap in F08. Existing snapshot/replay remains valid; it cannot be the only eventual means of live native sharing. Pointer equality, ptrtoint/address hashing, function dispatch, liveness and allocator behavior remain part of defined observations.

## Alternatives and witnesses

Native nodes at 0x10000 and 0x18000 form a cycle with two aliases to the second node's interior value. A size-only bump replay at device heap 0x2000 produces different addresses and pointer-derived results. Blind byte copying leaves native pointer words unmapped. A registered same-address profile preserves those observations where layouts/ranges agree; a different mapping needs a published observation-preserving representation, including integer-derived pointers and dispatch. A native free/reuse can change liveness/generation without changing loaded bytes, so byte-only snapshot caching is unsound. [verify.py](verify.py) checks aliases and metadata/epoch invalidation, not an actual native-memory backend.

## Recommended exact dialect diff

Append to §19.5 in both future tracks:

```text
The existing fixed non-PIE shared-address comparison/production construction
is profile fixed-shared-address-1. Retain all range/alignment/nonoverlap
checks and common addresses for values crossing the boundary. Bind each
selected program/root to its concrete layout, build record and memory
profile; co-packaging distinct layouts does not merge them.

Other native layouts require an explicit versioned mapping/relocation
profile covering globals, data/function pointers, indirect dispatch and
defined pointer comparisons/ptrtoint/address hashes. The profile preserves
those source observations; it cannot silently relocate unidentified
integer words or require native-only code/data in ISA memory. Revalidate
the complete linked device layout and profile after linking/rebinding.
```

Append to §8.5 and cross-reference §§17.1–17.3:

```text
Device-allocation replay and native registration are different entry-state
sources. For a registered native graph, acquire a consistent entry epoch
under the memory profile before reading bytes or metadata. Compute the
complete byte and metadata dependency closure (§19.5), including aliases,
cyclic/interior pointers, allocation-validity/address observations and
logical shadow-stack dependencies even when the byte read set is empty.

Canonicalize allocation identities without merging distinct live objects;
map all pointer observations by the selected profile. Import bytes,
bounds/liveness, pinned/ownership state and required allocator/shadow-stack
metadata before any private oracle allocation/access. Do not require native
malloc to have followed the device bump history or infer live logical
frames from backing-storage containment. The private construction must
produce the same defined entry observations, allocation/fault outcomes and
addresses as the corresponding materialised native closure. Any host-
compatible allocator/escaping-storage profile is explicit, not an unchecked
conversion of a private device address to a native pointer.

Retain §8.5's private L memory semantics: writes are restored/discarded
except declared outputs, input regions remain unchanged, and no native
load at a coherent address is introduced. Device bump replay keeps its
existing allocation order, invalidation, register unpreparation and charges.
An alternative native import needs published exact metadata/address and
allocation rules; these are required mechanics, not permission to promote
all native callers or narrow their allocation graphs.

Snapshot reuse requires identical dependent bytes AND allocator/liveness/
generation/shadow-stack metadata and entry mapping epoch. Relevant native
free/realloc, logical stack changes or rebinding invalidate it even if the
byte read set is unchanged. New host edits between closed launches are
part of the next entry epoch. Export/return/close obey the memory profile;
private L state is not blindly written back as shared host mutation.
```

The exact arbitrary-layout/allocator wire realization remains a versioned publication obligation under [native-sharing](native-sharing.md); the fixed profile is an admitted first construction, not the final capability boundary.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Fixed source images/layouts affect object/closure/build hashes; runtime registration epochs/mappings bind invocations and private entry-state identities. Metadata changes can invalidate cached snapshots without changing source bytes. Container/root/layout identities must agree with certificates and comparators. No ref recipe edit is inherent; if the accepted import adds ordinary materialised helper/replay rows, their bodies and costs become part of the actual device/reference closure. Driver/plugin/runtime/VM/checkers pin that published realization.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Fixed-address compatibility; noncontiguous cyclic native allocations; interior aliases and address-sensitive outputs; function pointers; shared globals; incompatible pointer width/layout; colliding ranges; metadata-only invalidation; repeated host edits; private L restoration; stack backing versus logical extents; escaping allocation and native free/realloc. Compare from the same entry identity/address configuration rather than relocated independent baselines. [INDEX](INDEX.md) records finite alias/epoch checks; no pointer relocation or allocator implementation is claimed.

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

Next versioned dialect revision plus 0.3 draft; default companion/**0.10** memory semantics. ISA 0.9's layout/bump model cannot silently acquire arbitrary native allocations. An in-version K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including coherent import and allocation semantics. Suggested decision `MIXED-ENTRY-MEMORY-PROFILES`; no unversioned catalog change.

## Mixed fat object

Use [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F15/F16. Each marked device program/root has a concrete layout/certificate/context `[]`, budgets/ledger and run/tape, while native main/argv/envp/libc and unrelated state stay native. Explicit mappings and allocator obligations preserve live native sharing rather than replacing it with universal promotion. These amendments never gate rungs 3/4; F30 stays deferred.

**Mixed fat-object scope (§§9–10, §12.3; K88).**

A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
§3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
lowering or omit mixed execution.

**Fixed-image mixed-object binding (K85; §§9–10).**

A native binary may carry VM objects only for marked
modules. The host launch manifest selects a VM entry and records the
classical image preparation/copy into its in-region, with resolved shape
and raw-byte hash. The certificate inventory is that entry's selected VM
closure and reached contexts, not the whole native executable. Image
preparation may come from host database construction or a declared
snapshot. Host bytes are bound to device cells using the declared packing,
not host pointer identity. Unmarked main, argv/envp, libc and unrelated
code remain native. The native comparison invokes the corresponding
materialized marked entry over the same image and varying domain; it
need not treat the entire binary as the oracle. Runtime shims and build
configuration are recorded as required by the North Star.

VM objects contain the selected marked entries and their required transitive device closures. Code outside those closures remains native; shared callees may have both host and device variants. Native comparison uses the materialised entry and its entire materialised callee closure with the North Star §3.2 runtimes and arithmetic libraries.

**Native-model mixed-object binding (K86; §§9–10).**

A mixed binary's native host runs main, argv/envp,
unmarked libc and ordinary code. Only marked modules are VM objects;
the launch manifest selects their exported entries. A native gate model
belongs to the target used for that VM closure and its reached native
leaves, not to the host machine instructions of the whole binary.
Certificate inventory and phase/control reconstruction cover that selected
closure's invocations. Each VM schedule pins the target/model hash even
when several modules share the target. Host qreg handles and angle values
are marshalled through the published launch bindings, not reinterpreted
as model basis indices. A native comparison uses the corresponding
materialized marked host entry and captured inputs; it does not require
every host instruction to have a quantum gate model.

The selected VM closure includes transitive callees of marked entries, whether or not those callees contain markers; native comparison materializes that same callee closure with the prescribed runtime and arithmetic libraries.

**Resource contexts and canonical displays (K87).**

For exactly one invocation context, resources and gates_used report its
§8.2 scheduled event accounting. resource_contexts may be omitted; if
present it MUST have exactly one matching record. The root context_key
is []. A standalone verified resource report binds one invocation and
an effective §11 schedule, including an explicit empty-entries schedule
when defaults supply all decisions. A schedule-free reference claim
retains K13's reference-only meaning and is not target-resource evidence.

For more than one invocation context, resource_contexts MUST contain
exactly one record for every §12.1 context, in increasing bytewise order
of the JCS encodings of their complete keys. Include default-covered and
predicated-off invocations. Full identity includes resolved source
point, loop indices, fixed-binding instance, forward semantic-call
occurrence and oracle step/phase where applicable, not just variant and
inherited-control count. Resolve each context's own args, token budgets,
placements, cleanup, epsilon shares, schedule/default decisions, native
leaves and wire intervals. Recompute every declared resource and gate
count from that context. gates_used counts native leaves after scheme
expansion, each once, with existing scheme/error attribution unchanged.

For compatibility, unkeyed resources/gates_used/estimate MUST be the
first canonical context's projection. An optional estimate's presence
and value agree with that projection. The projection discharges only
that context. Missing, duplicate, reordered, foreign or mismatched
context records, or a conflicting projection, reject the requested
report. A singleton array, when present, follows the same validation.

Whether or not estimate is present, accounting uses exact nonnegative
rational arithmetic. Each resources value is a canonical decimal string
using §10.1's 34-significant-digit upward encoding. For q>0, find integer
e with 10^e <= q < 10^(e+1), then display
ceil(q / 10^(e-33)) * 10^(e-33). Zero is "0". Spelling is ASCII digits with an optional decimal point; no exponent, no plus sign, no leading integer zeros except the value "0" itself, no trailing fractional zeros, no negative zero.

The checker compares the producer's canonical string with the canonical
encoding of its independently recomputed exact rational value, not the
numeric displayed decimal with that exact rational. Noncanonical strings
or differing encodings reject. Resource-row keys and class qualification
retain §8.2/§10.1's vector and escaping requirements; no scalar sum
replaces a required per-class row. Capacity, matching, objectives and
resource calculations continue to use exact rationals. Never add rounded
child displays to compute an exact parent value.

When estimate is present, precision_digits is 34 and its reduced
exact_resources fractions MUST also equal the recomputed values, with
nonnegative numerator and positive denominator decimal strings and zero
as 0/1. K12's existing mandatory-estimate cases and complete class rows
remain mandatory. Optional estimate here does not weaken K12. Omitting
estimate elsewhere does not mean a decimal must be an exact rational
representation; it is the defined upper display enclosure.

The root counts its realised event list once, including semantic callee
leaves. Child reports are independently checkable subtree/footprint views,
not extra costs added to the root. Measure child timing relative to that
invocation's entry, preserving internal delays in the effective schedule.
Restrict caller-owned interface and inherited-control lifetimes to that
invocation interval when displaying the child view. This neither
transfers ownership nor creates an additional charge to the root.
Within each view apply §8.2 dirty-borrow replacement: the borrower replaces
the owner's charge for that wire during the borrowed interval. There is
no second aggregation by summing child peak/time maps. Error-bound
multiplicities retain their existing separate composition rule.

For a non-K12 resource report, omission of estimate remains valid when displays match recomputed encodings; K12 estimate certificates still require estimate.

Closed-schema certificate readers require the K87 resource_contexts and canonical-display upgrade; legacy repeated-context flat reports require recomputation rather than an inferred max or sum.

**Resource-context witness (K87).** Let flip(t) have body x t, and let a root keep mutable t and in-role c live throughout:

```text
action_call @flip (t) () () ()
qpred c
action_call @flip (t) () () ()
qunpred c
```

The call keys name entry:0 and entry:2, with iteration=[], instance=[] and call_index=0. Under a native X with time and occupancy rates 1 in class fast, uncontrolled and controlled children have (time, occupancy, peak) (1,1,1) and (1,2,2); root values are (2,4,2), and X counts are 1,1,2. The second child occupies root time [1,2) but reports invocation-local time 1. A zero control value does not remove any charge.

For native duration 1/3, child times display as "0.3333333333333333333333333333333334" and root time as "0.6666666666666666666666666666666667"; root occupancy 4/3 displays as "1.333333333333333333333333333333334". These apply with or without optional estimate; when present, exact_resources.time for a child is {"numerator":"1","denominator":"3"}. Summing rounded child displays rejects. One wire live over [0,2), borrowed over [0,1), at owner rate 1 and dirty rate 1/4 has occupancy 5/4, not 9/4.

**Resource-report mixed-object binding (K87; §§9–10).**

A native host binary can embed VM objects only for
marked modules. A launch manifest selects an exported VM entry and its
schedule/target; that selected VM invocation is the resource-report root,
with context_key=[]. Its certificate inventory covers its VM closure and
all generated invocation contexts, not every native entry or libc routine.
Unmarked host main, argv/envp and ordinary host work remain native. A host
shim supplies the bound handles, args, images and budgets before launch.
Only costs within the bound VM realisation, including effects/transfers
actually represented by that contract, enter its vector. Separately
reported host/launch overhead must have an explicit scope and is not
silently claimed as included. Native comparison selects the corresponding

## 14. Formal model

### 14.1 Reference interpreter *(normative)*

Executes an oracle's reference body per basis configuration: locations
and cells are bit-vectors of their declared widths (no upper bound,
§3.3), pseudo-locations read-only, predication is
`if`, each §4.2 row is its classical function (including the inherent
`inrange` check of loads and stores, the activation records of §3.7, the
`alloctab` and stack arrays), `fault` and a triggered `lift.faultif`
record their effective cause (§5.4) and halt, `lift.
retval` halts with success and `f(x) = result_val`, the step counter
counts executed rows (on budget exhaustion exactly B rows have
executed), counters are unbounded integers. It **defines** F_B
and the budget unit and rejects any body that executes a non-permutation
gate.

For CC/R, `fault C` has the terminal-record semantics of §4.9 and the
logical history of §7.4: it preserves the complete executed prefix for
eligible reversal, including activations that have not returned. This
is T4's history traversal, not the Q machine's B inverse steps. In an
oracle body or lproc the same spelling remains the §4.2 fault row;
§3.6 and §5.2 supply its step-machine inverse and T2 prefix reversal.

### 14.2 Quantum state semantics

The state of a Q region is a vector in ⊗ over live wires in allocation
order (bundles little-endian). `qalloc` tensors |0⟩; `qfree` removes
clean factors; `qborrow` introduces a factor in unknown state possibly
entangled with a reference system R, and the region's operation must be
U ⊗ I on it (§5.6); gates act by §4.4 under the active predicate;
`measure` (CC) is the projective computational-basis measurement with
Born probabilities, consuming the wires.

### 14.3 Theorems

- **T1 (totality).** For every well-formed oracle, budget and x ∈ D the
  reference interpreter halts within B + 1 rows and F_B(x) is unique.
- **T2 (prefix reversal).** For every executor whose realisation is a
  composition of ε = 0 permutation gates implementing §5.2 steps 1–4
  with the inverse step of §3.6, on every x ∈ D every non-interface wire
  is clean after step 4 and borrowed bundles hold their initial values.
- **T3 (permutation theorem).** If the realised unitary V of a circuit on
  span(D) is a permutation matrix and the final basis tuple equals P(x)
  for every x ∈ D, then V|span(D) = P.
- **T4 (round trip).** For every finite CC/R trace with retained run
  history and tapes that is eligible for reversal under §10 — a
  `COMPLETED` or `FAILED` run containing no `measure` frame and no
  `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified — the history
  traversal of §7.4 restores the active environment, memory, allocation
  table, device-model state, the ledger entries of every register the
  run created or touched, token validity and active history (§7.4) to
  their entry values; audit records may remain; observed external
  events are not retracted. If, in addition, every region call is undone
  by the exact inverse of its realised forward operation (ε = 0
  realisations, or a realisation that is applied as its own adjoint),
  the quantum state of every register the run touched is restored
  exactly; otherwise to within the sum of the realisations' declared
  bounds (§12.3).
- **T5.** As §6.3.
- **T6 (executor agreement).** Two executors conforming to §3–§7 on one
  target with the same schedule produce on every x ∈ D the same final
  tuple for every oracle (exact) and, for every action with `operator_
  norm` inclusive bounds e₁, e₂, unitaries within e₁ + e₂ in operator
  norm; for channels, diamond bounds d₁ + d₂.

### 14.4 Mechanisation *(deferred)*

**Suite H / §11.1 K77 adapter vector.** Captured `oracle_invocation`
follows the source call's arguments and body/template capture, irrespective
of unrelated apply-site context; nested calls restore the enclosing context
on exit. K77 conformance includes the same §16.3 `inc8` closure and
positional vector `[x,t,f]` captured with B=2 and B=3, forwarded through
`invoke`/`call` and captured body/template adapters under a conflicting
apply-site context. Nested calls use their own explicit tuples. Assert
frame counts 2 and 3, final `(t,f)=(x+1 mod 256,1)` from zero seeds,
exact inverse restoration, and retained forward `call_index` values during
inverse traversal (0/1 for the two calls within one source-row expansion;
separate source points each start at 0). Reject absent, malformed or
source-inconsistent records before concrete expansion. This vector tests
the existing K77 binding rule; it adds no catalog argument or binding rule.

**MCX operand and phase witnesses (K93; suites C/F/T6).**

For s:wire, b:wire[2], t:wire, mcx (s, b) t resolves to [s,b[0],b[1],t], with k=3 and four gate operands. It maps (s,b,t) to (s,b,t XOR (s AND [b=3])) with phase +1 on all 16 basis inputs.
mcx (b, s) t resolves to [b[0],b[1],s,t]; it has the same unitary and K78 primitive identity as mcx (s, b) t, while retaining its distinct canonical source bytes.

For a:wire[2], s:wire and an active predicate r disjoint from these operands, mcx (s, a) t has own controls [s,a[0],a[1]], k=3 and total control count c=4; its target toggles exactly when r=s=1 and a=3. Check the expanded operand order as well as the truth table.

For the action swap p q; mcx (c) p with c:wire[2], the complete map is (p,q,c) -> (q XOR [c=3],p,c); its adjoint applies MCX before SWAP. For p=q=0, the action maps (|0,0,0,0⟩+|0,0,3,1⟩)/√2 to (|0,0,0,0⟩+|1,0,3,1⟩)/√2, in tuple order (p,q,c,R), preserving both displayed amplitudes. Check the complete basis columns with amplitude +1.

The written bundle operand remains in source envelopes. The witness object hash db3d3f92a79077074901449d83532f1a0d82ef1621bd833adb7ad14d1aedd467 and action closure e38d5da64f9a0ae89a66243bcbe5a321cb807a5bbabf83952b63d3465dfab346 stay unchanged under this in-version repair; they identify bytes. Schedules that counted one control must be regenerated and certificates rechecked; unchanged source hashes do not validate old execution caches. Source adapters supply all flattened controls and k to the existing resolved-wire ref recipes; those recipe bytes and their catalog hash stay unchanged. Changed adapter leaves require regenerated leaf hashes and affected schedules.

Flatten within each marked device module's actual wire bindings; wires and certificate domains do not include native host code. Gate arity and budgets belong to that device invocation. Shared device helpers may be linked under several roots without making native main or host-only libc certificate regions. No launch, opaque-handle or tape format changes follow from this operand rule. The mixed fat-object scope statement's container/root and mixed-launch boundary additions remain required.

## 17. Decisions deferred to implementation, with defaults

Each item is one an implementer must decide to build; any decision is
conformant provided two implementations agree on the default below until
the item is revised. Items 1–5 are asserted non-semantic.

1. **Binary encoding.** Default: none; §16 text is the only interchange
   form.
2. **Gate-name registry.** Default: the names of §4.4 only; an unknown
   gate name is a load error.
3. **Further device models.** Default: the four of §7.3; an `effect` on
   any other device is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`.
4. **Mechanisation of §14.** Default: none; the reference interpreter is
   the oracle.
5. **Memory layout of the opaque C objects.** Default: opaque; only the
   listed functions touch them.
6. **Measurement-based cleanup** (§6.4): the normative fixup, its
   controlled variant and its certificate kind. Default: unitary cleanup
   (`reverse`); `capabilities.mbu` is `false`.
7. **Expansion library** (§11): the gate-level expansion below row
   level. Default: the library `ref` published by `bennett-isa-spec`
   (ripple-carry adders and comparators, linear `inrange` scans,
   one-hot address dispatch, clean scratch released in reverse order);
   a schedule that names no `expansion` binds `ref` at the ISA version
   in the object's header, with the full-library envelope hash published
   for that version in §11.1. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4]
   For a 0.9 object this is exactly
   `{"name":"ref","version":"0.9","hash":"5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197"}`,
   equally for omission and an explicit binding. [deps: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4, ISA-11.1]
   §11.1 fixes its schema, ordinals, scratch identities, empty removable
   sets and deterministic decomposition algorithms. [deps: ISA-11.1]
   The bound catalog is published as `spec/ref-0.9.json`. The worked
   `ctl` action fixture (zsw.4) is in
   `conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/`: `controlled.bisa`,
   `target.json`, `leaves.json`, `schedule.json`, `frame.json` and
   `certificate.json`. It contains one exact controlled-X leaf, starts
   at time 0 and ends at time 1, with two live fast-class interface wires
   and no workspace. Its README records derivation and the pinned 0.8
   compatibility check separately from the 0.9 bindings.
8. **Enclosure procedure for transcendental entries** (§12.1). Default:
   Taylor series of sin and cos to the first term whose Lagrange
   remainder bound is below 2^−(`precision_bits` + 2), argument reduced
   modulo 2π with an outward-rounded enclosure of π.
9. **Default `epsilon_share`** (§8.1, §11) when a schedule assigns none.

# Shared global state at native launch boundaries

Round-2 finding `bp-acm` requires a contract decision about the native caller
adapter. This document requests that decision; it does not adopt a new protocol
or claim that the bug is fixed.

Dialect 0.2.10 §4.3 requires one classical memory shared by H, CC, R and L
entry state. Section 19.4 preserves native H callers unless pointer-rich sharing
requires promotion. The mixed-object requirement `bennett-4ht` also preserves
ordinary native callers and multiple selected module entries.

The ISA 0.8 §10 C API supplies program/run ownership, typed arguments/results,
host requests, reversal and close/unload. Its published header has no operation
to bind, import or export a classical memory range. A layout image supplies
initial data, not shared native storage. Using a native pointer as an ISA
integer address does not connect the two memories.

The regression `tests/review_regressions.py --major M --case global-counter`
links the emitted host bitcode with the published VM library and tests three
observations on both LLVM 18 and 23:

| Native caller actions | Required output | Current output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Call `top` twice | `1 2 host_global=2` | `1 1 host_global=0` |
| Call `top`, set native `g=40`, call again | `1 41 host_global=41` | `1 1 host_global=40` |
| Call `top`, then another marked entry sharing `g` | `1 2 host_global=2` | `1 1 host_global=0` |

The first row alone could tempt a program cache. A cache still leaves native
`g` unchanged and cannot import the intervening H write. Separate caches for
entries also retain separate device copies of the same source global. Neither
is a fix for §4.3. Promoting every native caller would change §19.4 placement.

The requested decision is the supported classical-state bridge for this
boundary: may the producer publish a versioned copy-in/writeback service using
existing ISA host requests, or should the launch contract supply explicit
memory-transfer/binding operations? Existing integer arguments/results could
also carry bytes through a generated adapter, but that would still need a
published mapping and ownership convention, not an unchecked host-pointer cast.

Whichever route is selected, its publication must specify:

- Source global identity, selected component ownership and native/device
  address correspondence, including globals shared by several entries.
- Copy-in of current H bytes and copy-out of device writes, including aliases,
  with the §19.4 promotion rule for pointer-rich reachable storage.
- Program context lifetime, compatible component linking, and handle/token
  namespaces; a function-local static cache cannot decide these for the native
  container.
- Memory ownership while a run is active, writeback of completed or faulted
  prefixes, and how reversal restores the host-visible state before subsequent
  H mutation or reuse.
- The adapter service/schema or C API, and the component metadata that lets
  plugin, driver, runtime and executor independently implement the same bridge.

This requests the smallest bridge needed to implement the existing shared
memory semantics. It does not request whole-process-only placement, private
access to VM memory, a change to the ISA 0.8 inner object grammar, or a silent
ISA upgrade. The general mixed-object audit is deferred; the determining
blocker here is the concrete P0 loss of an ordinary shared scalar global.
